# Otos Requirements

Authoritative registry of **implemented** product and system behavior. A row's
presence means Current. Retired rows are deleted (git history remembers). Process,
workflow, and tooling rules live in AGENTS.md, not here.

Every behavior change updates the affected rows in the same PR. Paths in
**Implemented in** and **Tested by** must exist on disk
(script/requirements-authority-check.sh enforces this).

Column meanings:

- **ID** — stable identifier (ARC-* architecture/system, APP-* product surface,
  ENR-* enrichment, MED-* media/originals).
- **Requirement** — the behavior itself.
- **Acceptance example** — a concrete observable example of the requirement holding.
- **Implemented in** — owning source files.
- **Tested by** — executable proof: `` `OtosTests/X.swift#testSymbol` `` or
  `` `script/guard-x.sh#self-test` ``; or bounded debt:
  `unverified: <specific clause and why it is not yet machine-checked>`.

## Architecture And System

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance example | Implemented in | Tested by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ARC-001 | Otos UI implementation shall prefer SwiftUI. AppKit shall be used only when SwiftUI cannot provide the required capability or cannot meet verified responsiveness or performance requirements. | Given a UI surface is implemented, it uses SwiftUI unless the AppKit grid boundary (or another documented AppKit escape) is required for a verified capability or responsiveness need. | Otos/ContentView.swift, Otos/OtosApp.swift, Otos/Views/MediaGridBridge.swift | `script/guard-single-main-window.sh#self-test` |
| ARC-002 | Otos shall run unsandboxed. Library-root access shall use normal filesystem paths. App Sandbox, security-scoped bookmarks, bookmark schema, bookmark lifecycles, and sandbox-driven file access flows are out of product scope. | Given the app is built, the project does not enable App Sandbox and library roots are ordinary filesystem paths. | Otos.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj, Otos/Models/CatalogPath.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift | `script/guard-unsandboxed.sh#self-test` |
| ARC-002A | `script/smoke-run.sh` shall never terminate an in-flight import or reorganization. The journals `active_file_operation` and `active_reorganization_component` are the durable busy truth (not `log show`), read via `sqlite3 -readonly` under WAL; any sqlite error counts as busy, any row forbids `kill` except an import row in phase `abandoned`, which is cleanup owed to the next launch and nothing in flight (per-root output is diagnostic only), and `is_import_active` aliases `has_active_operational_work`. | Given an import or reorganization journal row exists (even for a different root), script/smoke-run.sh --jank refuses to start and its cleanup trap leaves the app running instead of kill; given sqlite3 returns busy/corrupt, the script also treats the state as busy; given only an `abandoned` import row, or no journal row, the script proceeds and may quit_app. | script/lib/operational-busy.sh, script/smoke-run.sh, Otos/Stores/CatalogStoreImportJournal.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStoreReorganizationJournal.swift | `script/guard-smoke-run-busy.sh#self-test` |
| ARC-003 | `Store` shall use one actor as the single owner of GRDB, migrations, serialized initialization, root runtime state, per-root namespace-mutation epochs, scan and device-enumeration leases, removable-source attach/quiesce/resume/detach state, scan persistence cadence, all catalog queries, and the durable operational busy predicate (`hasActiveOperationalWork` over `active_file_operation` ∪ `active_reorganization_component`). Pure SQL/mapping for scoped reads lives in the `nonisolated` gateways `CatalogQueryGateway`/`EnrichmentClaimGateway`; the actor keeps only `timedRead`/`timedWrite`, `RootRegistry` snapshot capture, and `namespaceMutationEpoch`/`filesystemDirty`/`ScanLeaseController`/`reorganizationLeases` atomicity. | Given concurrent startup, mutation, or namespace-mutation-lease calls enter an unloaded Store, they await one initial load, so a lease for a persisted root is never denied as `rootNotRegistered` before roots load, and a catalog that cannot load denies it as `catalogUnavailable`; given catalog behavior is implemented, `Store` owns SQLite/scanning/runtime catalog truth, exposes cheap typed change notifications plus Store-owned query APIs for fixed libraries, removable sources, descriptor-ordered IDs, scoped filter options, visible asset details, and enrichment candidates; given a stale filesystem scan completes after an independent import, reorganization, or filesystem mutation, Store may upsert observed facts but suppresses missing media/companion deletion and retains typed dirty scopes for follow-up reconciliation; given a stale device enumeration completes after a newer owner is current, Store rejects the stale write/reconciliation; SwiftUI receives final projected catalog window state through `ViewModel`, and views do not call SQLite directly; given a Store-owned wrapper is called, it captures `roots`/`RootRegistry` outside `await db.read` and delegates pure SQL to the gateway so no scoped read can forget a scope dimension. | Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift, Otos/Stores/RootRegistry.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogQueryGateway.swift, Otos/Stores/EnrichmentClaimGateway.swift, Otos/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift | `OtosTests/CatalogStoreTests.swift#changeNotificationsAreCheapTypedSignals`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreLifecycleRaceTests.swift#concurrentStartupAndMutationShareOneInitialLoad`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreSchemaMigrationTests.swift#refusedCatalogDeniesNamespaceLeaseAsCatalogUnavailable`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreLifecycleRaceTests.swift#staleCallbacksCannotMutateSamePathReplacementScan`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreRemovableSourceTests.swift#staleDeviceEnumerationCannotWriteOrReconcileNewerRows`, `OtosTests/RootRegistryTests.swift#scopePredicateHonorsAllMediaAndLibraryScope`, `OtosTests/CatalogQueryGatewayTests.swift#orderedAssetIDQueryReturnsNilForScopeSelectingNothing`, `OtosTests/ScanLeaseControllerTests.swift#enqueueSkipsActiveAndDuplicatePendingPaths` |
| ARC-004 | Library-root domain identity shall be the normalized filesystem path for the current root location, with symlink components resolved for existing selected fixed roots before persistence; removable sources may additionally carry a stable source identity used to remap the current path. SQLite rowid may be used only as a private foreign-key implementation detail. | Given a root is shown, selected, logged, or compared as product state, code uses `LibraryRootPath`; given a user or smoke script selects a symlinked directory such as a Desktop OneDrive shortcut, Store persists the resolved provider directory path so scanner enumeration and root-relative paths use the same spelling; given an older reachable saved root is symlink-spelled, Store repairs it to the resolved path during startup before applying runtime root state and migrates any existing thumbnail path/file to the normalized-root deterministic name; given the same removable source identity returns at a new mount or staging path, Store updates the root path for that source; given media rows reference a root in SQLite, they may use `library_root.id` only inside persistence internals. | Otos/Models/CatalogModels.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift | `OtosTests/CatalogStoreRootNormalizationTests.swift#addLibraryResolvesSymlinkedRootBeforeScanning`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreRootNormalizationTests.swift#startupResolvesSavedSymlinkedRootBeforeScanning`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreRootNormalizationTests.swift#startupMigratesThumbnailPathsWhenNormalizingSavedRoot`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreRemovableSourceTests.swift#removableVolumeRemountsByIdentityAndStaysOutOfAllMedia` |
| ARC-005 | CatalogWindowViewModel shall own the single window's final UI state, explicit sidebar-selection intent delivery, platform removable-source monitor lifetimes, startup device-staging sweep handoff, per-camera catalog tasks, and automatic sidebar selection through SidebarSelectionPolicy until the user explicitly chooses a source. | Given a sidebar row is chosen by the user, the ViewModel sets `userHasChosenSource` and sends the intent through one owned query-descriptor stream; given startup completes before any user sidebar click, the ViewModel keeps a restored browser place's scope if it still exists (APP-043) and otherwise applies `SidebarSelectionPolicy.defaultSelection` once (connected removable/camera by most-recent attach, else single fixed library, else All Media); given a removable volume or camera attaches after the first loaded state, the ViewModel jumps to that source through `SidebarSelectionPolicy.selectionOnAttach`, keyed on the Store's attachment generation so only a real attach moves the user; given a stale projected state proves the currently selected root was removed, the ViewModel applies `SidebarSelectionPolicy.selectionOnSelectedSourceRemoved` instead of leaving a dead selection; given removable volume or camera events arrive, the ViewModel routes them through the same lossless catalog mutation or Store-owned task path; given camera monitoring starts, stale device-staging directories are swept off the MainActor before ImageCapture monitoring begins; given the ViewModel ends, it cancels its state, monitor, camera, staging-sweep, and selection/mutation consumers before finishing their continuations. | Otos/Services/CatalogProjection.swift, Otos/Services/SidebarSelectionPolicy.swift, Otos/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift | `OtosTests/CatalogWindowViewModelDefaultSelectionTests.swift#startupSelectsSingleLibraryWhenAllMediaIsHidden`, `OtosTests/SidebarSelectionPolicyTests.swift#defaultSelectionPrefersConnectedRemovable`, `OtosTests/SidebarSelectionPolicyTests.swift#selectionOnSelectedSourceRemovedMatchesDefault`, `OtosTests/SidebarSelectionPolicyTests.swift#selectionOnAttachSelectsANewlyAttachedCard`, `OtosTests/CatalogWindowViewModelDefaultSelectionTests.swift#insertingACardAfterTheUserChoseALibrarySelectsTheCard`, `OtosTests/CatalogWindowViewModelTests.swift#removedSelectedLibraryResetsSidebarSelectionToAllMedia` |
| ARC-006 | Catalog root sorting, fixed/removable source splitting, descriptor-ordered asset querying, sidebar-scope filtering, media kind/format filtering, title lookup, selected-root validation, availability hiding, device enumerating empty state, and empty-state selection shall run outside the MainActor. | Given a store change, sidebar selection change, sort change, filter change, removable attach/quiesce/resume/detach, or camera enumeration change occurs, CatalogProjection asks Store for descriptor-ordered IDs and computes CatalogWindowState off the MainActor before CatalogWindowViewModel assigns UI state. | Otos/Services/CatalogProjection.swift, Otos/Services/CatalogWindowPolicy.swift | `OtosTests/CatalogProjectionTests.swift#windowStatePreservesLibraryOrderAndSortsAssets` |
| ARC-007 | Catalog source roots shall store the smallest current source metadata needed to distinguish fixed libraries, removable volumes, and MTP cameras: kind, optional deviceIdentity, optional raw display name, and optional lastSeenAt. A source's identity is a `DeviceIdentity`, the one owner of its persisted text and its transience: a volume by its UUID, or by its mount path when it reports none; a camera by its persistent id, or by its name when it reports none; an identity from a mount path or a name, or one disambiguated by mount path, is transient. | Given Store loads roots, `LibraryRoot` carries source kind and display identity separately from transient availability/activity; given a removable source is attached, Store records or updates `device_identity`, `display_name`, and `last_seen_at`; given two mounted removable volumes report the same UUID, runtime source identity is disambiguated with the mount path without adding a durable fingerprint or source table; given Projection builds state, fixed libraries and removable sources are split by `kind`; given schema is inspected, no separate source table, durable scan ID, content fingerprint, identity sidecar, or app-owned original copy is introduced for removable media. | Otos/Models/CatalogModels.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift | `OtosTests/CameraSourcePolicyTests.swift#duplicateRemovableVolumeIdentitiesArePathDisambiguated`, `OtosTests/DeviceIdentityTests.swift#eachConstructorWritesTheTextTheCatalogPersists`, `OtosTests/DeviceIdentityTests.swift#onlyMountPathNameAndDisambiguatedIdentitiesAreTransient`, `OtosTests/CatalogProjectionTests.swift#removableSourcesAreSeparateAndExcludedFromAllMedia`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreRemovableSourceTests.swift#removableVolumeRemountsByIdentityAndStaysOutOfAllMedia` |
| ARC-008 | Catalog domain identifiers shall be typed at app boundaries. | Given code models a library root path, media relative path, or media asset identity, it uses LibraryRootPath, MediaRelativePath, or MediaAssetID instead of bare strings or concatenated string identifiers across app boundaries. | Otos/Models/CatalogModels.swift | unverified: typed identifiers at every app boundary need behavioral boundary coverage; current proof is limited to the implementation boundary |
| ARC-009 | Filesystem scans shall be idempotent reconciliation operations, not durable domain entities. Otos shall not store durable scan IDs, scan-history tables, resumable scan records, first-seen markers, last-seen markers, missing-since markers, or tombstone state. | Given a scan completes, currently observed rows are inserted or updated and missing rows are deleted for that root; given a scan is cancelled or fails, previously cataloged rows are preserved; given Store guards callbacks with a process-local path, private root-row, and in-memory lease generation, that concurrency bookkeeping is neither persisted nor exposed as durable scan identity; given schema or docs are inspected, no durable scan identity or scan history is represented as product state. | Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift | `OtosTests/CatalogStoreTests.swift#completedLaunchScanDeletesRowsForMissingFiles`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreTests.swift#failedLaunchScanDoesNotDeletePreviouslyCatalogedRows`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreTests.swift#cancelledLaunchScanDoesNotDeletePreviouslyCatalogedRows`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreLifecycleRaceTests.swift#staleCallbacksCannotMutateSamePathReplacementScan` |
| ARC-010 | Catalog root rows shall combine durable root/source identity with separate transient unknown, available, or unavailable availability and scan/enumeration activity. LibraryRoot shall remain durable identity and source metadata only. | Given a database-loaded fixed root's folder is there, its row reports `available` and its rows show before any scan starts, and given its folder is gone, `unavailable` (one `stat` at load, ADR 0008); given a newly added root has not yet been checked, its row reports `unknown`; given a removable source detaches, its row reports `unavailable` without deleting rows; given a camera is enumerating, its row reports enumeration activity separately from availability; given a view renders roots, it receives `[CatalogLibraryState]` without joining separate root, availability, activity, or ejectability collections. | Otos/Models/CatalogModels.swift, Otos/Views/SidebarView.swift, Otos/Views/SettingsView.swift | `OtosTests/CatalogStoreAvailabilityTests.swift#aReachableSavedLibraryShowsItsRowsBeforeAnyScan`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreAvailabilityTests.swift#anUnreachableSavedLibraryLoadsUnavailable`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreRemovableSourceTests.swift#quiesceKeepsRemovableSourceAvailableForSidebar`, `OtosTests/CatalogProjectionTests.swift#mtpCameraSelectionShowsEnumeratingStateAndConnectionBanner` |
| ARC-011 | Settings library and removable-source management shall consume projected root rows and catalog intents only; the Import tab receives only ImportPreferences bindings/closures. Settings may render availability/last-seen facts but shall not consume scan status, media assets, browser state, raw catalog state, plan payloads, or the whole catalog window ViewModel. | Given Settings renders the Libraries tab, `SettingsView` receives fixed `[CatalogLibraryState]` rows plus add/remove closures; given it renders Removable Media, it receives removable source rows plus a Forget closure; given it renders Import, it receives preferences plus a save closure; it displays name/path/availability or last-seen facts and does not display scan activity or receive `ViewModel`, media assets, or sidebar state. | Otos/Views/SettingsView.swift, Otos/OtosApp.swift | `script/guard-view-passivity.sh#self-test` |
| ARC-013 | Main-window catalog state shall distinguish sidebar scope, query descriptor, grid identity structure, visible tile detail invalidation, and visible tile detail reads with precise names. | Given code stores the selected sidebar row, it uses `sidebarSelection`; given code publishes the media browser state, `mediaGrid.descriptor` identifies the resolved sidebar scope, media filter, and sort order, `orderedAssetIDs` defines display order, and `detailRefreshGeneration` invalidates visible detail reads without carrying per-asset values; given cells need current values, the AppKit grid faults visible details by ID through the Store-backed `MediaTileDetailReader` (ARC-059) instead of receiving a full asset map. | Otos/Models/CatalogModels.swift | unverified: state naming and visible-ID detail reads need behavioral coverage; current proof is limited to the implementation boundary |
| ARC-014 | Otos shall have exactly one main window as a permanent product and architecture invariant. Multiple main windows shall not be designed, documented, proposed, deferred, or kept open as a future architecture path. | Given the app scene is inspected, the main catalog window uses singleton SwiftUI Window("Otos", id: "main"), the main scene does not use WindowGroup, and the standard New Window command is replaced or unavailable for the main window. | Otos/OtosApp.swift | `script/guard-single-main-window.sh#self-test` |
| ARC-015 | SwiftUI views shall not orchestrate catalog snapshot streams or projection invalidation. | Given ContentView is inspected, it renders CatalogWindowViewModel state and binds controls, but it does not subscribe to catalog notifications, call CatalogProjection, call CatalogStore, coordinate .onChange catalog work, or define view-owned update(snapshot:) catalog behavior. | Otos/ContentView.swift | `script/guard-view-passivity.sh#self-test` |
| ARC-016 | CatalogWindowViewModel shall be a MainActor adapter and explicit intent/task owner only, not the catalog truth owner or projection scheduler. | Given code is inspected, the ViewModel owns named startup, state-consumption, query-forwarding, and mutation-forwarding tasks, final SwiftUI state assignment, synchronous sidebar/sort/filter intent submission, scalar stall correlation, and local query-control state that is not rolled back by stale projected states; public add/ensure/remove intents create no task or direct Store call; SQLite, filesystem traversal, scanning, dirty cadence, sorting, filtering, availability hiding, and selection resolution remain in `Store` or `Projection`. | Otos/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift, Otos/Services/CatalogProjection.swift | `OtosTests/CatalogWindowViewModelQueryIntentTests.swift#staleProjectedStateDoesNotRollbackToggledBackQueryIntent`, `OtosTests/CatalogWindowViewModelTests.swift#startIsIdempotentAndAddRemoveIntentsRouteToStore` |
| ARC-017 | The regular-file catalog scanner shall skip macOS package descendants by default, record skipped package diagnostics, and retain a per-root skippedPackageCount from the last completed scan for later import-footer attention. Package-container media support requires an explicit future media-container decision. | Given a library contains Photos Library.photoslibrary with a file inside plus an ordinary file beside it, the scan completes, catalogs the ordinary file, does not catalog the package descendant, records catalog.scan.skippedPackage with the package path and available content type identifier, and Store reports a positive skipped-package count for that root. | Otos/Services/CatalogScanner.swift, Otos/Stores/RootRuntimeState.swift | `OtosTests/CatalogStoreTests.swift#scanSkipsPackageDescendantsAndRecordsDiagnostics` |
| ARC-018 | `Store` shall expose cheap typed catalog change notifications instead of a UI snapshot publication stream, and all catalog reads shall go through Store-owned query APIs. A change to existing facts emits `.changed(CatalogChangedFacts, rows:)`, naming the assets whose rows it changed or removed when the write knows them (`CatalogChangedRows`: an enrichment batch, a metadata re-read, an edit, provisional thumbnails, a scan or listing batch and a scan's or resync's deletions name theirs, a deletion with the Live Photo partners it leaves (`AssetRowDeletion`); a root's own state — its order, a scan or listing starting or ending — names `none`, and `named` always names at least one row (`CatalogNamedRows`); a root's availability moving, which changes which rows are in reach, is announced by the one function that sets it, unnamed; any other write is unnamed), and the kinds of fact the write touched — rows shown, capture dates, kinds and formats, grouping identifiers, choices and stack keys, locations, place names, faces, copies (digests, render signatures, pairs kept together), On This Day's search labels, what search read of a row (`searchContent`: an embedding or text reading written, which a description waits for), stored images and the edit they show (`images`), the bytes' record (`mediaRecord`), what can change a stack (`stacks`: a date, kind or grouping of a still in a burst or sharing its folder with a still it could pair with), detail — as the Store knows them at the write: an enrichment batch compares what SQLite stored before and after each row, and a write of many kinds (a scan, a root, a Trash move, an import) names `.all` (ADR 0005). Projection advances `detailRefreshGeneration` for every change, and repeats the ordered-asset read, the filter-options read, the stack candidates' read (`stackInputs`: rows shown, choices, `stacks`), the stack collapse and a shown search's match (keyed also by `PhotoSearchIndex.revision`, which moves with every change to the index's memory; its fact matches read rows shown, capture dates, locations and faces, `searchFactInputs`, only for a query naming facts) only when a kind of fact each depends on was touched since its last answer, which it keeps with the revisions its pass started from (`CatalogProjectionReads`); the options are read after the window publishes (`docs/research/2026-11-14-scope-switch-and-background-laps.md`). Duplicates and People are each asked for a build only after a publication that took up a change of a kind their answer is built from (`DuplicatesProjection.inputs`: rows shown, grouping, copies, kinds, capture dates, the record, choices, images; `PeopleAssignment.inputs`: faces, rows shown, grouping, kinds, capture dates, choices, images), so a faces write never rebuilds the groups and a digest or render signature never re-runs People (`docs/research/2026-11-14-derived-owners-and-face-checks.md`). A scan, device-listing or source-listing batch that only inserted rows (`CatalogRowChanges`, `ScannedFileUpsert.inserted`) emits `.assetsAdded` — and, for a new row a source's digest makes a copy of one the catalog holds, also `.changed([.detail, .copies])` naming it, which Duplicates and People take up: the rows carry nothing derived yet, so Projection publishes them without asking Duplicates or People for a build — it asks People only for its unchecked tally (`PeopleAssignment.requestTally`, one count read republished with the last state), since each new still is one not yet checked for faces — and `PhotoSearchIndex`, `PlaceNameReader`, `PreviewFaceReader` and `RenderSignatureRefresher` do not read for it; a batch that changes, hides or deletes a held row emits `.changed(.all)` (`docs/research/2026-11-12-listing-batch-cost.md`). Each owner of a derived product — render signatures, preview faces, place names, the search index, descriptions — wakes only on a change of a kind its own candidate SQL reads, derived from its statements' regions (`CatalogOwnerInputs`, over `CatalogChangedFacts.writes`, the one table of the columns each kind stands for), less the kinds its own writes name and with which roots are reachable; rows only added wake none of them, and a change of the user's locale wakes the place name reader. | Given Store content changes, subscribers receive a lightweight invalidation event; given a library is successfully removed, the lossless event also carries its typed root path so Projection can reset a selected root even if same-path re-add coalesces before publication; given UI state is needed, `Projection` asks Store for current root rows and ordered asset IDs, then increments a scalar detail-refresh generation so detail-only writes are not equality-suppressed; given an enrichment batch changes no capture date, kind, format, grouping identifier or location, Store names its detail (and the record, faces and copies it read) and Projection refreshes visible detail without reading the order or the options; given a new person (a faces write), People is rebuilt and the duplicate groups are not, and given copies kept together, the groups are rebuilt and People is not; given a batch moves a capture date, Projection reads the order again and not the options; given copies kept together under a library scope, neither is read again; given a choice (a stack fact), the order is read again and the options are not; given faces read under a library scope, the order is not read, and given a face given the person shown, that person's order is; given a search shown and a lap that took up only a choice, the index is not asked to match again, and given the index's memory moved, it is; given visible cells need details, the grid asks Store for those IDs only; given a listing batch of new rows, Store emits `.assetsAdded`, the window shows them and the duplicate groups are not rebuilt, while a batch that changes, hides or deletes a held row emits `.changed(.all)` and they are; given a new listed row whose digest another copy holds, Store emits `.assetsAdded` and a copies change naming it; given a deleted Live Photo still, the change names its movie; given a root lost by a scan that failed, it is announced unnamed, apart from the scan's own `none`, which wakes no owner of a derived product. Given a place-name write, a duplicates fact and a library added, each owner rings for exactly the changes that touch its inputs and never for its own writes; given a locale change, the place name reader wakes; every column an owner's SQL reads is named by a kind of fact. | Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift, Otos/Services/CatalogProjection.swift, Otos/Services/CatalogProjectionBuildTypes.swift, | ARC-019 | CatalogProjection shall own one publication loop for CatalogWindowState, driven by a doorbell and two revision counters: every intent and Store change adds to `requested`, and a pass advances `covered` to the `requested` it started from only when its build succeeds, so a request made while a pass runs is never lost. Direct sidebar, sort, and filter intents ring the doorbell immediately, while Store-originated background changes coalesce on a roughly one-second cadence, with at most one projection publication running at a time. An intent whose descriptor the build in flight does not answer cancels that build, and an intent for another sidebar row cancels an owed filter-options read: superseded work publishes nothing even if it finishes, covers nothing and rings the loop for the new request at once: the pass never waits for the cancelled work to end, which may finish its cancelled read while the next pass runs (ADR 0005). The first pass of an intent answers every read Projection holds an answer for from that answer — the order and collapse of All Media, held while the user is elsewhere, and of the last other scope; the import batches; the place-name languages — even when a fact it depends on moved since; when one had, it publishes that window, covers nothing, and the next pass reads again what moved and republishes, so a return to a scope already read paints without a read. A store change never cancels the build in flight; teardown cancels it and it publishes nothing. The filter's options never hold the window back, nor the pass after it: a pass that owes them publishes the window with the last options read for the same row, or none, asks for them and covers its request; the options are read as their own work, one read at a time with their own revision counter, kept and republished when the read ends, and cancelled by an intent for another row. They are read with every copy of identical files and never depend on which file is a copy. The People gallery reads no order, stacks, search or options. A failed Store read publishes nothing: the last published state stands, the request stays outstanding, and the next intent or cadence tick retries it. | Given Store changes arrive rapidly, Projection coalesces them on the cadence, does not start a second publication while one is in flight, and reruns after the in-flight publication when a request arrived during it, including a Store change; given a user changes sidebar scope, sort, or filter, Projection rings the doorbell for an immediate publication; given a build's read throws, nothing is published from that call and the next intent publishes; given the last subscriber ends while a build runs, nothing is published; given an intent for another scope arrives while a build is parked, that build is cancelled and never published and the new scope publishes; given a store change arrives while a build is parked, the build publishes; given the superseded build ignores its cancellation, the new scope publishes while it is still parked and what it returns is never published; given a return to All Media from a library with no fact moved, the order is not read and every state published shows the held order; given a capture date moved while the user was in the library, the return first publishes All Media's held order, then reads the order once and publishes the moved one; | ARC-022 | Store-owned SQLite queries shall produce catalog display order and filter membership from one `CatalogQueryDescriptor`, and `Projection` shall publish descriptor-scoped ordered IDs without sorting full asset values or carrying a full value map. Projection asks Store for ordered IDs when a change can have altered them (ARC-018), and may ask Store for separate counts only to distinguish empty-state meanings when no assets are visible. Every scoped read — ordered IDs, cataloged count, visible count, filter options — shall resolve its `SidebarSelection` through the one `CatalogScopePredicate` produced by `RootRegistry`, so no read can honor one scope dimension and omit another; the predicate carries root membership, the import-batch stamp and the location constraint (ADR 0040), and a scope that requires a location joins `asset_metadata` so the ordered-ID read also carries each row's coordinate. Every order — date (v55), name and size (v69) — the stack candidates' folder walk the filter's types share, and the import batches' read walk indexes that carry every column their row tests read (whether a row is shown, and whether it is a Live Photo's movie), so a row is tested without its table row being read. A date order of a scope by roots alone re-reads, after changes that moved only capture dates of rows they name, those rows alone by key and merges them into the order it holds, which is then the order a full read gives (`CatalogOrderPatch`, ADR 0005). The order held for a scope answers every search over it: a search narrows the order after it is read, so typing, changing or clearing a search reads no order, and a scope's unsearched collapse is held beside its last search's. A scope that names its rows by a key — an import batch, a set of identical copies, a stack's members, one person's photos (found from the person's faces through `idx_face_person_photo`, each then by its key) — reads them by that key's index and sorts the few it finds, for its order, its filter's types, its counts and its place options alike, never by walking the date order, every row or every named place cell; its stacks are read from its own folders (ARC-062). A person, On This Day's day and a map area seek their rows only while the seek's candidates — the person's faces, the day's index entries, the located rows of the area's cells, counted from their indexes in the same read — times a measured ratio (40, 2 and 10, `SeekSize`) are fewer than the rows a walk of the order visits; past that they walk the order and test each row in it, and their stacks are every stack of their roots: the most photographed person, a day holding most of a library and a world-sized area read the walk's pages, not a page or more per row (`docs/research/2026-11-15-large-narrow-scopes.md`). | Given a publication, Projection asks Store for ordered IDs for the resolved descriptor and available roots; given all selected assets are hidden only because roots are unavailable, Projection asks Store for the selected scope's cataloged count and publishes `.noAvailableMedia` rather than `.noMedia`; given an active filter hides every visible item while unfiltered assets exist, Projection publishes `.noFilterMatches`, including when the scope is an import batch whose every row that filter excludes; given a scope selects no root at all, every read answers empty without querying SQLite; given a window state, `orderedAssetIDs` is the descriptor-scoped ordered visible structure and visible details are fetched separately by ID. | Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogSQLTypes.swift, Otos/Stores/RootRegistry.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogQueryGateway.swift, Otos/Services/CatalogProjection.swift, Otos/Services/CatalogWindowPolicy.swift | `OtosTests/CatalogQueryGatewayTests.swift#orderedAssetIDQueryReturnsNilForScopeSelectingNothing`, `OtosTests/RootRegistryTests.swift#scopePredicateHonorsAllMediaAndLibraryScope`, `OtosTests/CatalogBatchScopeTests.swift#everyScopedReadAppliesBatchMembership`, `OtosTests/CatalogBatchScopeTests.swift#batchEmptiedByFilterReportsNoFilterMatches`, `OtosTests/CatalogProjectionOrderingTests.swift#detailOnlyStoreChangePublishesRefreshAndChecksOrder`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreQueryPlanTests.swift#theWindowReadsTestRowsInTheirIndexesAndLaunchWritesFindTheirRowsByIndex`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreQueryPlanTests.swift#keyedScopesSeekTheirOwnRowsInsteadOfWalkingTheOrder`, `OtosTests/ScopeReachPlanTests.swift#aPersonSeeksTheirPhotosUntilTheyAreTooManyAndThenWalksTheOrder`, `OtosTests/ScopeReachPlanTests.swift#onThisDaySeeksItsDayUntilTheDayIsTooLargeAndThenWalksTheOrder`, `OtosTests/ScopeReachPlanTests.swift#aMapAreaSeeksItsCellsUntilTheyHoldTooManyRowsAndThenWalksTheOrder`, `OtosTests/PhotoSearchIndexTests.swift#aSearchAndItsClearReadNoOrder`, `OtosTests/ProjectionEnrichmentCoalescingTests.swift#captureDateWritesReadOnlyTheirRowsAndKeepTheOrderAFullReadGives` |

| ARC-033 | All overlay for an item shares one lifecycle: held while the item exists inside a tracked library root, purged in full only on proven deletion, removable-source retention (a card or camera not attached and unseen for 90 days is forgotten at launch through the same removal as Forget; detach stamps when it was last seen), or explicit library-root removal, and never on temporary absence. Asset identity is the filesystem path, with no durable identifiers, content fingerprints, generated UUIDs, or generated sidecars. A move outside Otos keeps its overlay when the move witness (ADR 0034) proves it: a newly seen path whose file identifier, size and creation time match exactly one row on the same volume whose own path is gone takes that row, children included. | ARC-041 | While the app is running, filesystem changes under a saved available library root shall schedule automatic reconciliation for that root using typed dirty scopes (an event's path is read in the catalog's spelling, so a root under `/var`, `/tmp` or `/etc`, which FSEvents reports under `/private`, matches its own events), acted on as events arrive by one pass per root that coalesces events landing while it runs: supported regular-file FSEvents reconcile only that path; trustworthy descendant directory/`MustScanSubDirs` events reconcile that subtree; directory rename events escalate to full-root reconciliation; a remove-shaped directory event whose path is absent under a still-reachable root reconciles that subtree as an empty successful observation (purging catalog rows and companions under the boundary); dropped, unknown, root-changed, symlink, and other unsafe coverage fall back to full-root reconciliation. | ARC-046 | Import planning shall produce an immutable ready plan from Store snapshots plus live destination occupancy with no UI or file mutation. `MediaTransferPlanner` is pure and single-pass: `destinationFolders(for:)` derives every folder a plan can touch from source facts plus policy before any I/O, `FilesystemNamespaceSemantics.enumerateNamespace` reads exactly those folders once, and `planImport` decides every candidate (base name and each suffix retry) from that namespace without further observation. Destination freeness uses `FilesystemNamespaceSemantics` (not catalog trust alone), absence is claimed only for an enumerated folder — an unreadable folder blocks its candidates instead of reading as free, and a folder a file makes impossible (the file holds its name or an ancestor's) blocks its candidates as `.destinationFolderOccupied` instead of being retried — and a selected regular-file candidate is preexisting only when its live byte size and filesystem-precision mtime match the shared expected destination mtime. Naming is `ImportNaming`, the one owner Reorganize shares. Original-name imports preserve each physical member's exact filename and extension, a companion's extension spelling included; date/time naming gives a companion its kind's extension and uses the content type's Apple-preferred filename extension, preserving the source extension only when the type has no usable media preference. Import-plan staleness keys on two per-root revisions, applied by role. `namespaceRevision` — which paths the root occupies — advances only when the occupied media namespace actually changes (asset added/removed/renamed/resized, companion inventory, import/reorg settlement, foreign occupant), and invalidates a plan reading that root as source **or** destination. `namingRevision` — what Otos would call the root's assets — advances when an enrichment write changes `capture_wall_compact` on an import source (a card or camera), and invalidates a plan reading that root as **source only**, because the source capture wall is what the date/time destination name is derived from; on a library the write advances only `planRevision`, which reorganization reads, and on an Immich account nothing. The Store wakes the import coordinator only for a root an import plans against and only when its namespace or naming revision moved (`CatalogStore.recordNamingChange`, `LibraryRootKind.isImportPlanned`). An enrichment write on a *destination* renames nothing on disk and is never read by `fetchDestinationOccupants` (`relative_path`, `byte_size`, `content_modified_at_unix` only), so it never invalidates a plan copying into that root — planning settles while a large cloud destination keeps enriching. Identical rescans advance neither. Store publishes a lossless `RootPlanningChange` stream carrying both revisions; the coordinator subscribes before its first refresh, so no change falls between the two, and applies each by role and treats `planRevision`-only wakes (no-op rescans) as no-ops. Events during an active scan retain typed dirty scopes in the next dirty generation without planning-churn deferrals. | Given a source with capture-wall facts and an empty destination, the planner emits `.copy` under year/month + `YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS` naming with Apple's preferred extension; given original naming, every media and companion member retains its exact source name; given matching live size and expected mtime at the selected path, disposition is `.skipPreexisting` even without a catalog row; given a foreign occupant or mismatched/unknown live fact, the bundle advances suffix; given sources spanning three capture months, `destinationFolders(for:)` returns exactly those three folders and every chosen destination — suffix retries included — falls inside them; given a destination folder that could not be enumerated, its entries are `.blocked` and no copy is planned; given a destination folder that does not exist yet, every candidate in it is free; given no usable date, the entry is `.blocked(.noUsableDate)`; given repeated no-op `planRevision` bumps on the destination, the ready plan stays ready without a rebuild, while a real namespace bump forces a replan; given the destination library enriches and its `namingRevision` advances repeatedly, the ready plan keeps its generation and is not rebuilt, while a destination `namespaceRevision` bump does rebuild it; given the source's `namingRevision` advances, the plan is rebuilt because the copy's name is derived from that fact; given safe file events while a scan lease is active, Store accumulates `.file` dirt and settles after the lease without an unconditional full-root follow-up. | Otos/Services/MediaTransferPlanner.swift, Otos/Services/ImportDestinationDate.swift, Otos/Services/FilesystemNamespaceSemantics.swift, Otos/Services/MediaOperationCoordinator.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift, Otos/Stores/FilesystemReconciler.swift | `OtosTests/MediaTransferPlannerTests.swift#matchingLiveFactsSkipEvenWhenDestinationIsNotInCatalog`, `OtosTests/MediaTransferPlannerTests.swift#declaredFoldersCoverEveryChosenDestination`, `OtosTests/MediaTransferPlannerTests.swift#unreadableDestinationFolderBlocksInsteadOfCopying`, `OtosTests/MediaTransferPlannerTests.swift#blocksWhenNoUsableDate`, `OtosTests/MediaTransferPlannerTests.swift#companionNamesFollowThePolicy`, `OtosTests/MediaOperationCoordinatorTests.swift#noOpPlanRevisionChurnDoesNotDisturbReadyPlan`, `OtosTests/MediaOperationCoordinatorTests.swift#destinationNamingChurnDoesNotDisturbReadyPlan`, `OtosTests/MediaOperationCoordinatorTests.swift#sourceNamingChangeReplansBecauseItRenamesTheCopy` |

| ARC-047 | Attached removable sources get per-source plans/state and can queue Import. Plans revalidate occupancy before publication and cancel on detach. A change recorded while a build runs — a revision, destination or preference change — is never cleared by that build's completion: a plan request is a counter (`planRequested`/`planCovered`) that only taking a build up covers, so the session re-plans after it. Import intents (destination, session preferences, Import, Cancel, Try Again, preference changes) reach the coordinator through one lossless ordered stream with one consumer in the view model, so "change the destination, then Import" imports into the new destination, and Import returns when the intent is applied rather than when the copy ends, so Cancel is never held behind it; execution progress travels in one per-run newest-value stream that is drained before the run's result is reported, so no sample follows the settlement. Session state changes only in `ImportSessionMachine.handle(_:)`, a synchronous transition fed build, execution, refresh and intent events, each result dropped unless its typed identity still owns the session. Device (MTP) enumeration batches and completion advance the same root occupancy revision as a filesystem scan, so a plan built before live device inventory landed is marked dirty and rebuilt once it does — not left permanently stale. A published plan survives the rebuilds that replace it: the session keeps the last plan it proved and keeps presenting it while a newer build over the same proven set is in flight, so a refresh never reads as a first check — except when the rebuild's inputs changed: after a run has settled, the plan it executed from is dropped because that run made it false, and whenever the destination changes — a pop-up pick, or a default following a library reorder — the held plan and the settled outcome are dropped because their counts were measured against the previous library — the bar returns to Checking (with its naming count, when a metadata wait was the last thing it proved) until the plan for the new library lands, and never presents the old plan under the new library's name or offers an Import whose number the click would not honour. A run's outcome is a recorded fact, not a phase: completion, stop and failure are each stored on the session and survive the post-settlement rebuild; the phase says only what the session is doing now. A failed run states the count its executor published before it failed, never a later catalog read. A proven plan carries the library it was proven for and the run that built it, and a running import carries its own destination, progress and batch proof, so name claims and settlement are about the library the plan was built for, and a run releases that library — not the source's destination at settlement — to the rebuilds other sources owe it. A build that could not read a destination folder is deferred and retried with a bounded backoff while the last proven plan keeps presenting; it is never published as a plan (ADR 0019). It is not presented while naming facts are pending — rows enrichment has not read are not described by the held plan, and reporting its count would under-report them. The session does keep the last **naming progress** it proved and presents that across a rebuild, so a re-plan during preparation never drops the bar's count or its determinate track; only an attachment that has never reported progress draws a countless Preparing. The session keeps one last proof — a plan, a settled run or a naming count (`SourceSession.lastProven`) — and each replaces the one before it, so a count from before a plan is never shown after it, not even while the card is read again and the held plan cannot be offered. A source's plan is offered as Ready and Import becomes enabled only once `ImportSourceReadiness` (ADR 0024) proves the source's own live scan/enumeration has settled to idle — never while it is still scanning/enumerating or unreachable, and never as a provably-empty card until the current plan is guaranteed to reflect the settled occupancy. A plan whose build was taken up while the source was still being read is never offered or run: once the source settles it is rebuilt, whether or not the read moved a revision and whether the source settled before or after that build finished; `op=import.attachToReady` measures only a plan that can be offered. Import uses MediaImportExecutor, which never refuses a run for want of destination space — it copies entries in plan order up to `FreeSpaceFloor.bytes` and stops as a designed, resumable outcome (ADR 0027) — deletion settles through the journal; auto-eject occurs only after complete success. The post-import auto-eject fires the ViewModel's eject intent — the one the sidebar's eject control fires — so it drains Otos's own readers, retries a busy volume, and surfaces a failure the same way (2026-09-13: it had skipped the drain and dropped the outcome). The import destination is remembered per removable source (`destinationBySource`, the machine's one record of it, loaded before the first attachment is seen and changed only through one writer) and is read back on every new attachment, including a reattach of a source Otos has already seen — an explicit pop-up pick or a still-tracked default alike resumes exactly where it left off. First sight of a source, or a remembered library that is no longer available, falls back to the first available library in the user's own Settings → Libraries order (`library_root.sort_index`) and starts tracking it; a destination still on that tracked default keeps following reorders and reattachments — a change made while a run executes is held until that run settles, then applied — while an explicit pick survives an ordinary refresh until it actually becomes unavailable. Store assigns each removable attachment a process-local generation and the coordinator publishes it with the source presentation. A source's presentation exists only while its card is attached: on detach its outcome — whatever it was — is logged (`op=import.outcome phase=settled`) and discarded, never kept for another row. The executing task is owned and drained after detach, so its eventual journal count can settle the old batch without mutating a replacement attachment at the same path. | Given a source attaches with a library, it reaches Ready; given a real occupancy change forces a rebuild of an already-ready source, every presentation published during that rebuild is still Ready rather than falling back to preparing; given a plan was proven or a run settled before the card gained rows whose naming facts are pending, every presentation published during the metadata wait — rebuilds and stale builds included — is the naming count, never Ready or Done; given a plan proven after a metadata wait, a rebuild while the card is read again shows Checking with no count; given attach after startup, planning stream reaches Ready; given zero new items with delete, source items are deleted; given execution fails, source remains; given blocked entries remain, cleanup is withheld; given a stale build after detach, no presentation appears; given a card is detached during or after a run, no presentation for it remains; given the user reorders libraries while a card is attached and still on the default, the destination follows the new first library; given the reorder happens while the card's import is executing, the running copy keeps its destination and the re-plan after settlement follows the new first library; given a destination was picked via the pop-up, it survives a reorder that doesn't affect its own availability; given a source with an explicit or default destination is detached and later reattaches, it resumes at that same remembered destination instead of resetting to the first library. | Otos/Services/MediaOperationCoordinator.swift, | ARC-048 | Filesystem import shall journal each copy in `active_file_operation`, stage via `copyfile(3)` with byte progress/cancel on the copy queue (`CopyfileTransfer.offCooperativePool`, a dispatch queue of its own, never the cooperative pool, which eight blocked copies fill on an eight-core Mac), hash the completed primary staging file in the same eight-worker import operation, durably persist the digest plus `.readyToPublish` before exclusive publication, stamp destination mtime from capture-wall or source mtime, publish with the exclusive-publication ladder (`renamex_np(RENAME_EXCL)` / hard-link / check-then-act), durably persist `.publishedPendingSettlement` after the rename, and settle the digest plus actual destination size/mtime atomically with journal clearance under pragma. Journal update/delete zero-row mutations fail. Every staging file an entry creates in the library — the primary's and each companion's — is journaled on the entry before the file exists, so no staging file is ever unnamed by a record and no directory sweep exists. An entry that ends without publishing removes its staging files and then its row; a file that will not go keeps the row in phase `abandoned` (not work in flight) for the next launch. Settlement clears the row. A landed entry's card file is erased on its own settle (ADR 0027) by one ordered consumer per run, under the card's namespace lease and journaled in `active_source_erase` like the sweep; a card another owner holds keeps the file for the end-of-run sweep, which runs under its own lease (APP-032). `MediaImportExecutor` owns one run's filesystem lifecycle; `MediaImportRecovery` owns earlier processes' journals and runs once at `MediaOperationCoordinator.start()`, before the coordinator runs any import, so a running import is never treated as a crash to recover. Recovery judges each row only after the Store's initial load has registered persisted roots; it removes every companion staging file the row names (recovery never publishes a companion), then follows the phase/final/staging matrix for the primary: final-plus-staging and exclusive destination races are foreign, final-only recovery requires size/integer-second mtime/SHA-1 proof, dataless or unknown finals retain the journal for deliberate materialization, and published finals without a journal row are left for the next plan. `Store` owns semantic durable transitions and atomic catalog settlement, and performs no import filesystem cleanup. Each planned companion is copied through the same byte path into its own hidden staging file beside its destination, after the primary's hash, and published exclusively after the primary; only a companion that landed is recorded with the primary, with the facts of the file that landed. A companion that did not land (copy refused, destination taken) is not recorded, its source is never erased, and its item counts as not copied (an incomplete entry failure the re-plan offers again); a cancel or a full volume during a companion stops the entry like the primary. | Given a ready plan with one `.copy` entry, Import creates a journal row, copies and hashes a hidden adjacent staging file, durably records readiness, publishes exclusively, durably records pending settlement, settles a canonical digest and `import_batch_unix_ms`, and clears the journal; given final and staging both exist, recovery removes staging only and records foreign; given a matching journaled final-only file, recovery settles it, while a mismatch retains the source; given a published journal with a digest, recovery settles it only after ownership proof; given an `.xmp` companion that copies, it lands beside its primary, is recorded paired to it, and Erase removes its source; given a companion whose copy fails or whose destination was taken, the primary lands, the companion is not recorded or overwritten, its source survives Erase, the run reports the item as not copied and incomplete, and no staging file remains; | ARC-050 | Library reorganization planning shall be a pure, non-mutating planner that applies the import naming policy through the same `ImportNaming` owner import uses: naming bundles (one stem key, ARC-045 — a RAW and its JPEG) share one date and one collision suffix, suffixes are the import policy's `_001`, `_002`, and companions are named after their primary. The runner reads exactly the folders `destinationFolders(for:)` returns, once, and the planner decides every candidate from that namespace in one pass. A bundle stays where it is when it is undated, when a file holds its folder's name or an ancestor's (`folderOccupied`), when its folder could not be read (`folderUnreadable`), when it is already named as the policy names it, or when no suffix is free; staying members keep their names and blocked members never enter the move graph. A moving bundle takes the first suffix whose names are not kept by a staying bundle, not taken by an earlier bundle, and not held by a file the catalog does not know. All path comparisons use the volume name key; renames are ordered so none lands on an occupied slot, and a cycle or a case-only relabel steps aside through one temporary name in its own folder, built from the member's catalog path. Duplicate final lookup keys make the plan inconsistent and it is never run. | Given two files that only differ by case on a case-insensitive volume, the rename steps aside through a stable temporary name; given a .HIF item identified as HEIF under date/time naming, the destination extension is .heif; given two sources competing for a name a staying file holds, they take `_001` and `_002`; given a RAW and its JPEG whose name is taken, both take the same suffix; given an undatable item, only that media is `.blocked(.noUsableDate)`; given a file holding the destination folder's name, the item is `.blocked(.folderOccupied)` and nothing moves; given a swap, one member steps aside once. | Otos/Services/ReorganizationPlanner.swift, Otos/Services/ImportNaming.swift, Otos/Services/FilesystemNamespaceSemantics.swift, Otos/Services/FilesystemNamespaceSemantics+NameKey.swift, Otos/Models/ReorganizationModels.swift, Otos/Services/LibraryReorganizationRunner.swift | `OtosTests/ReorganizationPlannerTests.swift#caseOnlyRelabelStepsAsideThroughAStableTemporaryName`, `OtosTests/ReorganizationPlannerTests.swift#dateTimePolicyUsesCanonicalAppleExtension`, `OtosTests/ReorganizationPlannerTests.swift#competingSourcesTakeImportSuffixesAroundAStayingFile`, `OtosTests/ReorganizationPlannerTests.swift#aRawAndItsJpegShareOneSuffix`, `OtosTests/ReorganizationPlannerTests.swift#undatableMediaIsBlockedAndStaysOutOfTheMoves`, `OtosTests/ReorganizationPlannerTests.swift#aFileHoldingTheFolderNameBlocksTheItem`, `OtosTests/ReorganizationPlannerTests.swift#aSwapStepsAsideOnce`, `OtosTests/ReorganizationPlannerTests.swift#caseInsensitiveCollapsesSpelling` |
| ARC-051 | Library reorganization execution shall journal each dependency component in normalized `active_reorganization_*` tables before the first move, hold one typed root reorganization lease for the run (deferring scans/events; dirty-only reconcile at release), execute same-volume no-overwrite steps with forward/ambiguous recovery, and settle identities with a set-based two-phase private-key remap that preserves overlay columns and thumbnail path references, recording one pending `CatalogIdentityRemapBatch` per settled component in the settlement transaction. A component is atomic against the library: one that cannot finish (destination taken, folder unavailable, move refused, journal or settlement write failed) is rolled back — completed steps moved back newest first, folders it created removed once empty, journal deleted — before the lease is released, and the run stops there with earlier components settled. A rollback that cannot restore a file marks the component blocked and keeps the journal and the lease. From the moment the Store loads, every root with a journaled component is held under the reorganization lease, so nothing scans, reconciles, imports into or trashes from a half-moved library before its recovery. Startup recovery, run once by the composition root after the Store starts, adopts that hold and resumes every journal: `prepared`/`moving` run forward and settle, `filesystemComplete` settles, `rollingBack`/`blocked` roll back; a component that reaches an end state releases its lease. A run executes only the plan revision the user confirmed; a changed library returns the new plan for confirmation and moves nothing. `ReorganizationExecutor` owns directory, move and rollback mechanics; `LibraryReorganizationRunner` owns execution and startup recovery decisions; `Store` owns scan-exclusion leases, semantic durable transitions, and atomic catalog settlement. | Given a swap component, the journal records the temporary hop and settlement remaps both media rows without PK collision; | ARC-053 | Import settlement shall verify a completed batch against durable `import_batch_unix_ms` rows and shall give the copy each product separately (ADR 0009): the source row's metadata when its metadata stamp (`metadata_source_size`, `metadata_source_mtime`) names the size and mtime the import copied, and its rendering — thumbnail and preview — when its rendering stamp (`rendered_source_size`, `rendered_source_mtime`) does, the same freshness rule enrichment applies to every row. | ARC-056 | Filesystem name equality shall follow the containing volume's probed case and Unicode-normalization capabilities through one `FilesystemNameKey` owner. Catalog reconciliation, import namespace/reservation planning, and reorganization shall retain physical spelling while comparing derived keys; a respelling updates the existing overlay row rather than purging it. Every write of a row's path, a scan's, an import's or a reorganization's, writes that path's key with it, made with the root's stored capabilities (`case_sensitive_names`, read in the writing transaction by an import's settlement and a reorganization's; no key-making write takes a default), and a scan rewrites a key it finds out of date, so a row is found only by the key of the path it holds. | Given a case-only respelling on a case-insensitive volume, the catalog keeps one asset and its overlay; given a case-sensitive destination, case-only names remain distinct; given import and reorganization planning, the same key rules prevent overwrite or duplicate claims; given a reorganized photo, a new file at the path it left is a new photo and the moved photo keeps its overlay; given a catalog with empty or stale keys, the v36 migration makes every key from its path; given an import of `2024/IMG_1.jpg` onto a case-sensitive root holding a Liked `2024/img_1.jpg`, the copy is a new photo and the existing one keeps its path and choice. | Otos/Services/FilesystemNamespaceSemantics+NameKey.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStoreReorganizationJournal.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogSchema.swift, Otos/Services/MediaTransferPlanner.swift, Otos/Services/ReorganizationPlanner.swift | `OtosTests/ReorganizationPlannerTests.swift#caseInsensitiveCollapsesSpelling`, `OtosTests/MediaTransferPlannerTests.swift#caseInsensitiveDestinationDoesNotPlanCaseOnlyCopy`, `OtosTests/LibraryReorganizationRunnerTests.swift#aNewFileAtAReorganizedPhotosOldPathIsANewPhoto`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreSchemaMigrationTests.swift#thePathKeysMigrationMakesEveryKeyFromItsPath`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreTests.swift#anImportOntoACaseSensitiveRootNeverTakesACaseTwinsOverlay` |
| ARC-057 | Import planning shall withhold a date-dependent plan while any source row's naming fact is unproven — that is, enrichment has not yet read the file's metadata (`metadata_source_size`/`metadata_source_mtime` absent) and no capture wall is stored. "No capture wall" on an unread row means "not yet looked at", not "none exists": naming from the filesystem mtime there asserts a fact Otos has not established, and because the destination name is also the dedupe key, the next plan computes a different name, finds nothing at that path, and copies again. A row is `.terminal` — plan now — once a capture wall is stored, or once enrichment has looked and found none, which makes the mtime a *proven* fallback rather than an assumed one. Readiness waits on the metadata stamp alone, never on the rendering or digest (ADR 0009). The naming question stays derived separately from the metadata staleness predicate, which answers whether enrichment should re-run: a stored capture wall is terminal even when the predicate can never mark the row current. A build that decides to wait revalidates against live rows before parking, because a batch landing mid-build is not replayed by the catalog-change wake; and a catalog change handled after the build was taken up — a batch landing after that live read — makes the parked session plan again at once. A source file whose bytes could not be opened is never treated as pending: it can be neither enriched nor copied, so it does not hold the offer open, the plan blocks it, and the bar surfaces it as an attention item rather than silently skipping or endlessly retrying it. The gate applies to every source kind including MTP cameras: a camera's bytes are staged locally precisely so they can be enriched, and `ICCameraFile`'s own dates are absolute instants that cannot yield a local wall clock, so a camera import offered before enrichment carries the same fabricated names as a card's. A camera item's scanned modification date is taken capture-first, since the modification-first precedence is end-of-recording for video. Enrichment that changes the capture-wall fact advances the root's `namingRevision`, which causes a re-plan of every session reading that root as its **source** — the capture wall names the copy. It does not advance `namespaceRevision`, so a plan copying *into* an enriching root is untouched (ARC-046). A row naming from mtime uses no fabricated sentinel. | Given a source row enrichment has not read, the coordinator publishes metadata waiting rather than Ready even though the row carries a filesystem mtime; given enrichment then reads it and finds no embedded date, the source reaches Ready and names from that mtime as a proven fallback; given enrichment supplies a capture wall for a row whose mtime is absent — a row the staleness predicate can never mark current — the source reaches Ready instead of waiting forever; given an enrichment batch lands while a build is in flight, the build revalidates against live rows and rebuilds instead of parking on a wake that will never arrive; given the batch lands after that read, the session parks and plans again in the same step; given a source file whose bytes cannot be opened, the plan blocks that entry as unreadable, the offer is not withheld on its account, and it is neither counted as copied nor as already present; given capture-wall enrichment changes, the next plan uses the updated name. | Otos/Models/MediaOperationModels.swift, Otos/Stores/MediaOperationGateway.swift, Otos/Services/MediaOperationCoordinator.swift, Otos/Services/ImportSessionMachine.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift, Otos/Services/ImportNaming.swift | `OtosTests/ImportNamingTests.swift#fallbackUsesTheSuppliedLocalTimeZone`, `OtosTests/MediaTransferPlannerTests.swift#policyThatNeverConsultsDateDoesNotWait`, `OtosTests/MediaTransferPlannerTests.swift#zeroByteMediaIsExcludedFromCopyAndNaming`, `OtosTests/ImportPlanningNamingFactsTests.swift#aKnownCaptureWallIsTerminalEvenWhenEnrichmentCanNeverSettle`, `OtosTests/ImportPlanningNamingFactsTests.swift#aDurableMtimeIsPendingUntilEnrichmentHasLooked`, `OtosTests/MediaTransferPlannerTests.swift#unreadableSourceIsBlockedNotSilentlySkipped`, `OtosTests/MediaOperationCoordinatorTests.swift#catalogChangeWakesWaitingForMetadataSource`, `OtosTests/ImportSessionMachineTests.swift#aCatalogChangeAfterTheBuildsReadIsTakenUpWhenTheBuildParks` |
| ARC-058 | A pending Move to Trash shall be a durable fact on the asset's own catalog row (`media_asset.pending_trash_batch_unix_ms`, schema v20), not a second table or an in-memory set. Every user-facing media query excludes pending rows through the shared scope clause, and import-history counts exclude them; the row and its overlay are kept until the file is proven gone, and a scan that sees a file vanish that the queue has not moved ends the intent by deleting the row. Once the queue moves the file to the system Trash, the row is kept — still hidden, no longer offered to the queue — with the reported Trash path (`media_asset.trash_path`, schema v10), is never claimed by enrichment, and is purged by a scan only once the Trash no longer holds the file (`ENOENT`); a file seen again at the row's path (Finder's Put Back, after a relaunch too, or undo) shows the row again with its overlay, and clearing a pending mark never shows such a row. The trash bar's Cancel withdraws every item still pending, read from these rows, so it works after a relaunch. The queue that drains pending rows treats a failed Store read as unknown, not empty: a pass whose pending-item read fails publishes no queue state and retries on a timer; a download size that cannot be read is unknown, not zero; and an item the user took back (restore, withdraw, immediate delete, undo of a pending request) while a pass held it in its snapshot is never moved afterwards. Shutdown ends the queue's work: a pass suspended when it lands starts no download and arms no timer, so no download outlives shutdown. | Given an item is marked pending, grid and count queries omit it while its file and row exist; ## Media And Originals

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance example | Implemented in | Tested by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MED-001 | User originals shall remain read-only by default. The sole current filesystem exception is explicit user-initiated Move to Trash, which uses recoverable system Trash; no sidecar write is permitted, its only byte access is the deliberate `MaterializationCoordinator` download of a dataless member that system Trash cannot otherwise accept, and permanent deletion (`FileManager.removeItem`) happens only when the user answers `Delete` to an explicit per-occurrence `Delete … immediately?` prompt for items that cannot reach the Trash (APP-027), never automatically. | Given a feature reads media roots, it does not delete, modify, rewrite, or move originals; given the user explicitly chooses Move to Trash, only catalog-proven members are passed to `FileManager.trashItem`, companions precede primaries, a dataless member is materialized first and is never passed to `trashItem` while dataless, `removeItem` runs only after an explicit `Delete` answer, and partial filesystem truth is retained. | Otos/Services/CatalogScanner.swift, Otos/Services/EnrichmentCoordinator.swift, Otos/Services/MediaEnricher.swift, Otos/Services/MediaTrashCoordinator.swift | `OtosTests/OriginalsReadOnlyTests.swift#addLibraryAndFullScanLeaveOriginalTreeUnchanged`, `OtosTests/OriginalsReadOnlyTests.swift#externalChangeRescanWindowDoesNotRewriteExistingOriginals`, `OtosTests/OriginalsReadOnlyTests.swift#companionInventoryAndRemoveLibraryLeaveOriginalTreeUnchanged`, `OtosTests/OriginalsReadOnlyTests.swift#enrichmentAndThumbnailStagingLeaveOriginalTreeUnchanged`, `OtosTests/OriginalsReadOnlyTests.swift#materializationCoordinatorLeavesOriginalTreeUnchanged`, `OtosTests/OriginalsReadOnlyTests.swift#previewDecodeLeavesOriginalTreeUnchanged`, `OtosTests/OriginalsReadOnlyTests.swift#videoPreviewLeavesOriginalTreeUnchanged`, `OtosTests/MediaTrashCoordinatorTests.swift#pendingItemsAreTrashedCompanionFirstAndTheirRowsLeave`, `OtosTests/MediaTrashCoordinatorTests.swift#aFailedMoveUnhidesTheItemAndReportsIt`, `OtosTests/MediaTrashCoordinatorTests.swift#datalessMembersDownloadAtCommandPriorityWithoutHoldingTheLease`, `OtosTests/MediaTrashCoordinatorTests.swift#aDefinitiveDownloadFailureAsksAndDeleteImmediatelyRemovesTheFiles`; unverified: provider-backed dataless materialization and production system-Trash runtime remain beyond the current harness cases |
| MED-002 | The app shall not fabricate user-visible or domain facts. | Given a date, path, provider state, progress value, count, or capability is unknown, the UI/domain model keeps it unknown or explicitly qualified. | Otos/Models/CatalogModels.swift, Otos/Services/CatalogProjection.swift | `OtosTests/CatalogProjectionTests.swift#windowStatePreservesLibraryOrderAndSortsAssets` |
| MED-004 | Otos is an image/video product and shall never add audio-specific playback, metadata, thumbnails, icons, classification, or workflows. | Given an audio file exists on disk under a library root, it is not cataloged by scan; given future media features are proposed, they do not add audio-specific product behavior. | Otos/Services/MediaEligibility.swift | `script/guard-no-audio.sh#self-test` |

| APP-001 | Otos documentation and user-facing summaries shall claim only behavior proven by current source, tests, and evidence. | Given current behavior is documented, it may describe implemented library management, automatic catalog scans, availability, grid browsing, toolbar/menu sort and filter, curation choices, recoverable Move to Trash, background thumbnail and capture-date enrichment, import planning/execution through the full import bar (`APP-029`), and import-batch sidebar history; it does not claim export, search, a metadata inspector, or editing behavior; loupe and compare preview are implemented. | Otos/ContentView.swift, Otos/Views/MediaGridView.swift, Otos/Views/ImportPlanFooter.swift, Otos/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelCuration.swift, Otos/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelTrash.swift | `OtosTests/MediaTrashCoordinatorTests.swift#pendingItemsAreTrashedCompanionFirstAndTheirRowsLeave`, `OtosTests/MediaTrashCoordinatorTests.swift#aFailedMoveUnhidesTheItemAndReportsIt` |
| APP-002 | Otos shall provide a SwiftUI sidebar with a shared row layout, optional All Media (only when 2+ fixed libraries exist), an On This Day row under All Media and under it a People row and a Places row, the ways to browse the whole library, whenever one fixed library exists (APP-069, APP-072, APP-057, ADR 0049, ADR 0047, ADR 0040; People and Places each highlighted too while a person's photos or a place under it are open; owner decision, 2026-11-12), one row for each fixed saved library in the user's own stored order, an Imports section for nonempty import batches (newest first, after Libraries and before Removable), each batch it lists counting the photos its grid shows, a RAW+JPEG pair or a burst imported together once (ADR 0052); only the batches the window shows are counted — the newest five, an older selected batch, and every batch while All Imports lists them (`ImportBatchListing`, `docs/research/2026-11-15-import-batch-photo-counts.md`), a separate Removable section for connected removable sources, and last a Utilities section holding only the Duplicates row (APP-060), which exists only while there are duplicates and so comes last, where its arrival moves no row. The import bar is drawn only while a removable source's own row is selected; selecting an import batch shows that batch and no bar, whatever state its run is in. While the bar is on screen, neither the card's row nor its batch row shows a status indicator; otherwise exactly one shows one — the removable row while the card is being prepared, the batch row while its run copies (DES-040). An import moves the sidebar selection from the card's own row to its batch exactly once, when the sidebar first draws that batch's row (after its first item has landed, even if the run has finished by then), never before the row is drawn, and never from anywhere else; a removable row that disappears while selected resolves to the ordinary default. Under the file types, the filter's People section lists every visible person with a name (favourites first; an unnamed one only while checked) and `Unidentified Faces`, each a checkbox; several checked rows are photos with all of them (ADR 0047); a person the filter holds who is hidden or merged away leaves it. The filter's choices — file types and places — are those present in the scope of the shown sidebar row (`SidebarSelection.sidebarRow`): a library offers only what it holds, never what only a card holds, and a leaf is offered its row's choices. Under People, the Places section lists every country Apple Maps named for the located items in that scope, most items first, each opening onto its towns, most items first (a country with some towns checked is mixed), every place the filter holds that nothing named has keeping a row — a held town under its country after the named towns, a held country after the named countries — and `No places named yet` before any is named; People, the countries and each country's towns show their first five rows plus any checked row past them, with `Show All N People` / `Countries` / `Towns` and `Show Less` in place, and the whole people or country list a search field once there are more than eight (`docs/design/people.md`, `docs/design/places.md`); a photo matches when its map cell is named one of the checked countries or towns in the language the filter holds them in, checking a country takes all its towns and unchecking one town of a checked country keeps the rest, and once the catalog holds no names in that language — the Mac's language changed and the old names were forgotten — every checked place leaves the filter, its saved place and the popover, since nothing the catalog stores names a place apart from its name (ADR 0051). Sort exposes Date — Newest First/Oldest First, Filename — A to Z/Z to A, Size — Largest First/Smallest First, and Likeness — Least Like Sam First/Most Like Sam First as eight mutually exclusive choices; the likeness orders name the person whose photos show and are disabled, never hidden, elsewhere, where the reads use the default order. | APP-024 | Each cataloged image or video has exactly one durable curation choice: Liked, Not Rated, or Disliked. New rows default to Not Rated; the overlay remains independent from selection, scope, media kind, import state, and filesystem facts, is preserved by import/reorganization row copies, and is purged only with proven row deletion or root removal. Neutral keeps its keyboard (`u`), menu, and VoiceOver paths. Curation is never color-only: the selected state carries the accessibility value `liked`/`not rated`/`disliked` once on the item — the grid tile itself (`MediaAssetTileView`) or the Loupe/Compare hover region (`MediaChoiceHoverRegion`) — rather than a redundant "Selected"/"Not selected" on each hover button, and Like/Mark as Not Rated/Dislike are all three exposed as VoiceOver custom actions on that same item rather than separate Tab stops (DES-068, DES-085).
