| APP-027 | Photo > Move to Trash, Command-Backspace, the toolbar Action menu, and the context menu item resolve the current Grid selection, Loupe focus, or active Compare pane and act at once, without a confirmation: the act is undoable from Edit ▸ Undo (APP-039), and the HIG reserves alerts for uncommon or irreversible acts. Plain Delete is unbound. The command hides the targets at once by recording a durable pending-trash intent on their catalog rows (ARC-058) and hands them to `MediaTrashCoordinator`, the queue owner, which resumes pending items after relaunch. Once hidden, the Grid selection or Loupe focus moves to the nearest survivor of the last hidden item in the order shown when the command was issued — the next item, or the previous one when the hidden items ended the list — and the Grid clears its selection only when nothing survives. For each item it moves only catalog-proven primary and `.xmp`/`.srt` members with `FileManager.trashItem` off MainActor, companions before primary, holding the root namespace-mutation lease only around the moves and rechecking under it that every member is still local, and reconciles only successful paths. A dataless cloud placeholder cannot leave its File Provider domain (`trashItem` reports `NSFeatureUnsupportedError`), so dataless members are first materialized through `MaterializationCoordinator` at `command` priority without the lease. A transient download outcome (timeout, provider unavailable, cancellation) leaves the item pending and retries with backoff. A definitive one (`requestFailed`), or a download that would leave the volume below the 5 GB `FreeSpaceFloor` (ADR 0027), asks `Delete “name” immediately?` / `Delete N items immediately?` with a destructive `Delete` and a default `Cancel`; same-kind questions merge. `Delete` removes the files with `FileManager.removeItem`; `Cancel` re-shows a failed download's items, and at the floor keeps them pending until a later pass finds room, without asking again. A move that fails after download re-shows the item, selects it, and shows the failure alert; in Loupe the re-selected item becomes the focus and its pane decode is requested, so the pane never stays on another item's spinner. Every per-path failure is logged as `op=mediaTrash.path phase=failed reason=…` with the `ErrorDiagnostics` fields (domain, code, description, path, underlying error, failure reason, and the userInfo keys present). Device/MTP rows are ineligible. | Given a cataloged primary has a paired companion, the companion is moved first and both rows leave the catalog; given a pending item, it is absent from media queries while its file exists; given the primary's move fails, the item reappears and the failure is reported, and in Loupe the pane shows that item's decode; given dataless members, they download at `command` priority while no lease is held; given a timeout, the item stays pending and later moves; given a definitive download failure, the delete question is asked and `Delete` removes the files without a trash call, while `Cancel` re-shows the item; given the floor, the question is asked once and the download starts when room appears; given pending items from an earlier session, a new coordinator trashes them; given a withdrawn request, the item reappears and nothing is moved; given Loupe on the last item, Move to Trash focuses the previous item, and on a middle item the next one; | APP-018 | MTP/PTP cameras shall attach as removable camera sources through ImageCapture, reject mass-storage devices, show catalog rows and provisional thumbnails from device facts (an item is cataloged at its parent folders and the name the device lists — `ICCameraItem.name`, unique in its folder, never `originalFilename`, which an iPhone repeats as `FullSizeRender.heic` for every edited photo; an item the camera reports no name for, or a path two items claim, has no path that would survive the next enumeration, so it is skipped and logged rather than given an invented one), route materialization by root kind rather than by matching a file path, stage bytes for enrichment and keep them for the life of the app session — an eject keeps them, because they are already off the camera and discarding them would re-download the whole device on the next attach. Staged bytes are cleared at exactly three points, each covering a case the others cannot: **per item the moment its copy settles durably in the library** (a failed or blocked entry keeps its bytes so a retry does not re-download them, and crash recovery releases the same way); **at quit**, in an unbounded window with a visible "Clearing cached camera files…" state, because `applicationWillTerminate` is bounded and macOS ends the process partway through — which is why tens of gigabytes survived a clean quit while a small test case did not; and **at launch** as the crash-safe backstop, which must run before camera monitoring starts and off the main thread. Staging trees are per process instance, so the launch sweep removes what an ended session left without touching a second running instance's in-flight transfer. A release path is proven to lie inside this instance's staging tree before anything is deleted; one outside it is refused and logged, never removed, and show a dismissable connection guidance banner when selected. Every ImageCaptureCore callback shall claim no isolation the framework does not give — delegate methods `nonisolated` on a plain (never `@preconcurrency`) conformance, and every completion/result closure passed to a `request…` API written so it inherits none — each hopping explicitly to its main-actor owner. ImageCaptureCore delivers some callbacks — `cameraDevice(_:didReceivePTPEvent:)` among them — on its own `ICDeviceOperationQueue`, and claiming isolation turns that into a runtime executor check that traps rather than a hop. The rule covers closures because it was first written for delegate methods only, and `requestDownload`'s completion then trapped the app the moment device staging began working (`_dispatch_assert_queue_fail` under `swift_task_checkIsolatedSwift`); `requestThumbnailData` and `requestDeleteFiles` carried the same latent claim. Owner state written by those hops is sticky or monotonic, so hop order cannot change an observed outcome. While a camera is connected its catalog follows it: every add, remove or rename the camera reports after its catalog is complete runs one more pass (newest-wins, passes never overlap) that records the list, deletes rows only when the list held still, and stages and thumbnails only items no earlier pass saw; given the device provides a provisional thumbnail, Store fills a missing thumbnail path without replacing a final thumbnail; given enrichment needs bytes, the request is routed by the asset's root kind — a `deviceMTP` root is served only by the active camera session, never by the cloud provider — and a device root with no live session answers `deviceUnavailable` rather than deferring to a provider that cannot serve it; | APP-022 | Loupe and compare shall show a filmstrip of the full current scope in display order — horizontal scroll, same square tiles, same selection gestures and no-blank paint as the grid — by parameterizing the existing AppKit collection bridge (`.grid` / `.strip`), not a second thumbnail engine. | APP-023 | Full-size loupe/compare decode shall live in `PreviewImageLoader`, which asks the shared materialization owner to make the original local at interactive priority — a dataless placeholder, or a camera original not yet staged, is fetched; a missing in-place original is not — rechecks before reading, coalesces decode work, and lets the ViewModel reject stale results by expected identity. A pane whose tile-detail read fails decodes and publishes nothing — a decode without the row would drop the photo's edit and curation — clears its spinner, and is asked again: a screen-sized request by the next reconcile, a full-resolution one by the next zoom report (ARC-059). | APP-030 | The grid shall group into day sections under Date sort only (`.bestDate` field, either direction) — `.name`/`.size` sort renders one ungrouped section with neither header nor bar, since a non-date-ordered list has no meaningful day grouping (DES-069). Each day section is headed by a `.headline` date (`RelativeDateText`: Today/Yesterday/Last `<weekday>`/absolute date) and a `.subheadline` item count that scrolls with its run, and an app-owned day bar at the top of the grid carries a copy of that label for whichever day owns the viewport's top edge, so a date is on screen at all times. The two are one label drawn twice — a single view, identical treatment, and the same rect, the header spanning the section's full width rather than its inset content width so the label never shifts between them; every drawn day label carries a DES-023 hairline at its bottom edge — a day separator under an in-flow header, and under the bar the contextual scroll edge macOS draws while content passes beneath a floating element, present only once the grid has left its top; the blank first header draws neither label nor rule, and the handoff does not animate because nothing moves; every day section draws its own in-flow header except the first, whose rect the bar already covers and which rubber-band scrolling past the top would otherwise slide out from under it as a duplicate; its space is still reserved. What a header draws is a function of the section it is in now and the days last applied, re-derived for every header on screen after each structural apply as well as when AppKit makes one, because a diffable apply keeps the header view of every section whose day survives however far it moves. The bar reserves no space and is shown continuously under Date sort — including at the top of the grid, where it rests exactly on the first header and draws the same label in the same rect, so leaving it on costs nothing visible. It is hidden only where there is no day to name. It is resolved on every scroll-bounds change rather than behind the scroll-work coalesce, which is a trailing-edge debounce that does not fire until scrolling stops. Where the viewport's top edge sits in the inset gap above a header, the bar names the preceding day, as a pinned header does. AppKit's `pinToVisibleBounds` is deliberately not used: it relocates every header of a section into a container repositioned from the main thread on each bounds change while tiles move on the compositor, and on macOS 26+ attaches an undeclinable scroll-edge blur of the passing tiles (`docs/research/2026-09-04-grid-day-header-pinning-and-scroll-edge-effect.md`). The bar's frame is constant, so it cannot separate from the content it labels. Assets with no known capture date still get their own section (`nil` bucket) rather than being dropped from the day structure or folded into the ungrouped case. Grouping is identical for a library, All Media, and a removable source. | Given Date — Newest/Oldest First, a scroll of mixed-day content names a date at all times and the count matches that day's | APP-032 | Explicit card Erase uses one immutable planner-owned manifest: it removes copied or preexisting media, media a library holds by content (ADR 0018) once the catalog re-confirms that holding at erase time — an available library row with the same current digest and no pending trash — useful sidecars, recognized cruft, and recognized source artifacts only after root-contained path validation; a copy is eligible only after publication, and unknown files remain untouched. Zero-byte media is excluded from copying but included in Erase. The terminal erase-only bar reports actual deleted counts, shows `Card erased · N items deleted` with Eject Card, and creates no import-batch selection. Source deletion has exactly one implementation, `SourceEraser`, which every caller routes through and which refuses fixed library roots as read-only. The erase sweep takes the source root's namespace lease for the sweep alone, after the copy phase; a lease denial reports an incomplete erase and never fails the import or claims the card was removed. Each candidate is journaled before deletion, and restart recovery settles every journaled entry for a root under one lease. The run's erase outcome counts every source it erased, whether as that entry landed (ADR 0027) or in the end-of-run sweep; the sweep leaves a path the run already erased alone, so an erased file is never reported as skipped. Each file the erase removes, or finds already gone, leaves the catalog as it goes: its catalog row and derived images are removed in the same durable write that clears its journal entry, so the card's catalog matches the card before it is ejected rather than after a rescan the eject usually cancels; recovery settles both together. A file counts as already gone only when its card is mounted and the path answers `ENOENT`; an unmounted card or any other error is a failed erase that keeps the row and the journal entry. | Given an erase-only plan, the source file is removed, its actual deletion outcome is rendered, the bar creates no import-batch selection, and unknown files remain; given a copy fails or is skipped as foreign, its source survives; given normalized-name collisions or unknown persisted volume capabilities, no row is destructively reconciled; given another owner holds the source root's namespace, the copies stand and the bar reports an incomplete erase rather than a failed import or a removed card; given the app quits mid-sweep, every journaled entry for that root is erased on the next launch; given an erase failure or skipped path, the bar reports an incomplete erase rather than a clean copy result; | APP-039 | Edit ▸ Undo (⌘Z) and Edit ▸ Redo (⇧⌘Z) shall revert and reapply curation choices — Like, Mark as Not Rated, Dislike — from every surface that submits them, and edits (APP-046), on the window's one undo stack. The inverse of a user choice is registered as the choice is shown (APP-086), and restores the prior choice per asset the Store reports from the write's own transaction, so a batch whose members held mixed values is restored per asset; an undo pressed before the write answers is written after it, from those priors. Only rows the write actually changed are restored; a choice that changed nothing takes its entry back off the stack when the Store says so. Undo and redo travel the same serial curation lane as user choices, never advance the selection, and register their own inverse synchronously so redo works after undo. An undo or redo whose items are not the current targets selects them — the loupe moves to the first, the grid scrolls to it — so a change is never made out of sight (`hig-validation.md` F-18); edits reveal their photo the same way. Menu titles name the act (`Undo Like`, `Redo Dislike`) and both items are disabled when their stack is empty. Move to Trash is on the same stack (`Undo Move to Trash` / `Redo Move to Trash`): undo withdraws items still pending (they reappear with no filesystem call) and moves items already in the system Trash back to their original paths from the location `trashItem` reported, never over an existing file, showing each kept row again with everything it held — choice, edit, import batch, thumbnail; an item that cannot be put back fails truthfully with the `This item couldn’t be put back.` / `Some items couldn’t be put back.` alert; redo hides the items again under a new command. `Undo Move to Trash` is registered as the items leave the window, before the Store records the move (APP-085); the mark, undo, redo and the answers to the trash prompts reach the queue through one lossless ordered stream, so an undo is always applied after the mark it undoes and before a redo that follows it. Trash undo entries live in memory for the session; after a relaunch Finder's Put Back returns an item with its overlay, because its row is kept while the file is in the Trash (ARC-058, ADR 0022). People's acts are on the same stack (APP-072), registered as each act is shown (APP-087): each undo or redo registers its opposite synchronously inside the manager's undo or redo, so redoing one of several undone acts keeps the rest to redo, and writes back the state the write before it replaced (`PeopleUndoRecord`), so a redone act returns the catalog to the state the act left — a person it created comes back under the same ID and later acts naming them redo too. | APP-043 | Otos shall reopen where the user left it. At quit the ViewModel saves one browser place — sidebar scope (every kind, Places and an area under it included), filter, sort, view mode, the selection in display order, and the item to bring back into view (the loupe's focused item, or the grid's middle visible item) — as a versioned preference value. At launch the first query uses the saved scope, filter and sort. Once the catalog has loaded its roots, a saved scope that still exists replaces the launch default, including a card connected at launch; one that is gone (including All Media when fewer than two fixed libraries exist) falls back to the launch default, keeps the saved sort, starts that row unfiltered (APP-013), and drops the rest of the place. When the first non-empty result for the saved query arrives, the surviving selection is restored, then the grid centers on the saved middle item, the loupe reopens on the saved focus, or compare reopens on the saved pair; a scope with one presentation (the Places map, the duplicates list, where a Duplicates group reopens) restores no view mode. | APP-046 | The edit panel (DES-092; shown and hidden by View ▸ Show Edit / Hide Edit, E and the toolbar button) shall edit the current target when it is exactly one image or RAW, with the Light section's Exposure, Contrast, Highlights (−100…100, positive lifting the upper tones through a tone curve) and Shadows plus Reset. Edit ▸ Undo and Redo restore recipes through the same lane and move the panel's values with them. A render that cannot happen hands the photo to enrichment, which renders with the saved recipe. | APP-048 | The edit panel's Crop tool (DES-092) shall crop, straighten, turn and flip the photo (ADR 0036). Photo ▸ Crop, C and the panel's Crop button turn it on, first showing the panel and switching to the loupe if needed. The loupe then shows the photo with every edit but its crop, with the crop drawn over it. The crop's frame is the original's upright size as the decode the photo is edited from reports it, never the catalog's. Dragging inside moves the crop; a handle resizes it about the opposite corner or edge; a constrained aspect keeps its shape and shrinks to stay in the frame. A drag saves one `Crop` undo step when let go and renders nothing while it moves. The tool starts with the aspect Custom, so a crop takes whatever shape suits the photo. Choosing an aspect fits the largest crop of that shape around the current center; Rotate Aspect turns it. A constrained aspect is portrait or landscape as the opened photo's upright shape is, until Rotate Aspect chooses. Done, Return, C, Escape (after a picker), leaving the loupe, another photo or hiding the panel end the tool. With Custom, Shift during a handle drag keeps the crop's shape. While the tool is on, its canvas holds first responder and returns it when the tool ends; arrow keys nudge the crop one frame pixel (ten with Shift) and a held key saves one step on release. Rotate Left (⌘[), Rotate Right (⌘]), Flip Horizontal and Flip Vertical save at once, with the crop turning or mirroring with the photo. Straighten (−45…45°, clockwise positive) rotates about the center and scales to keep the frame covered. The Crop section's rows stand in three groups, 12 pt apart (DES-092): Straighten with `Level`, Rotate & Flip, and Aspect with Suggested. `Level ▸ Auto` straightens the photo by its level reading as one `Auto Straighten` step (ADR 0045): the camera's gravity reading, which MediaMetadata reports and the photo's opening reads from the original, else GeoCalib's roll of the unedited photo when its uncertainty is at most 3°. The reading is reversed when the photo is flipped once, and the button is available only once a level is trusted. With the tool on, a line drawn on the photo — ⌘-drag, or a drag with the ruler armed (arming it turns the tool on) — turns it so that line is level, or plumb when drawn nearer vertical, as one `Straighten` step. `Suggested ▸ Crop` applies FocalNet's crop of the frame as saved, in any shape, as one `Auto Crop` step (ADR 0045). The frame is read when the photo opens and again when a turn, flip or straighten changes it; the button is available only for the frame the draft shows and only when FocalNet suggests a crop. Both read only the photo being edited: a read for a photo already left develops nothing. A cropped photo is developed from a frame large enough for its crop to reach the requested size. The pane's native size is the edited photo's, and the white balance picker samples the developed pixel under the click through the geometry. | Given a crop drag released, the recipe holds the crop and `Undo Crop` is offered; given Escape, the tool ends in the loupe; given leaving the loupe, it ends; given Rotate Right then Undo, the turns go 1 then 0; given four turns or two flips, the recipe returns to start; given a split red/blue photo, a turn puts red on top, a flip puts blue on the left, and a left-half crop is all red at 48×64; given straighten 12°, the size is unchanged and the corners are filled; given an iPhone back camera's gravity reading for −3°, Auto saves −3° as `Undo Auto Straighten`, and +3° once flipped; given that reading from a front camera, Auto never uses it; given a crop reading for the frame shown, Suggested ▸ Crop saves it as `Undo Auto Crop`, and a turn makes the button wait for the turned frame's reading; given a read for a photo no longer open, nothing is developed and the open photo still reads; given fields drawn from a known camera, the solver recovers its roll within 0.01°; given the ruler pressed with the tool off, the tool turns on armed; given a reading for another turn, or none, nothing changes; given a line drawn rising 4° to the right, the straighten is +4° as `Undo Straighten`; given a line nearer vertical, it is made plumb; given a half-width crop rendered at long edge 64, the output's long edge is 64; given a flipped photo, a picker click on its left half samples the original's right half. | | APP-051 | Copy, Share, Open, Open With and drag shall hand another app one full-size file per photo, as Finder does (ADR 0037): the original for an unedited still that is not a RAW or an untrimmed video; a full-size JPEG rendered with the recipe (keeping the original's Exif, GPS, TIFF and IPTC metadata, orientation reset) for an edited still or any RAW; the trimmed range, by passthrough, for a trimmed video. Prepared files live in Application Support's `HandOff` folder at a path fixed by the photo and its recipe, are reused within a launch, and are cleared before the first hand-off of the next. Copy, Share and Open travel one ordered lane: while photos are prepared a bottom status bar reads `Preparing N photos to copy` (or share, open) with M of N and Cancel (DES-094), counting only photos that take work — a file to render or copy, or a staged original to fetch, never an original that is there or a missing one no source can send; Cancel hands nothing over; a photo that cannot be prepared fails the hand-off with `Couldn’t Prepare Photos`, whose message says the disk is full when a write was refused for space (`FreeSpaceFloor.refusedForSpace`: Foundation's out-of-space code, POSIX `ENOSPC` in the error chain, or AVFoundation's `diskFull`) , names a source that could not send an original by its sidebar name — `“Family” isn’t answering, so one photo couldn’t be downloaded.` for an Immich account whose server did not answer, `“EOS R5” isn’t connected, so one photo couldn’t be copied from it.` for a camera — and says the original may be unavailable otherwise. An original a source stages and has not yet copied is fetched as the user's work before it is handed over as itself, and Open and Open With list apps for it by its file type. Reveal in Finder travels the same lane, as `Preparing N photos to reveal`, and reveals each photo's original — never a rendered file — fetching a staged original not yet copied first; a source that cannot send one fails it with the same alert ending `Nothing was revealed.`, and a cloud placeholder is revealed as it is, never materialized. | APP-060 | A Duplicates sidebar row shall exist, alone under a Utilities header, the last section of the sidebar, while any group of identical copies is active (and while selected), counting photos with duplicates; its list shows groups of visible fixed-library copies whose current digests match — in one library or account unless the user compares across them (APP-083) — read through the digest index alone, never grouping every row of the scope (DES-107, ADR 0042). Every group has a default: each library keeps its best copy (companions; for same photo the camera original, most pixels, largest file; an edit; a choice; no Finder/browser/import copy suffix; earliest birth; path order), and only a library's copies with work that cannot be combined — two edits, two with companions, two different choices — wait. Moving a copy carries its choice and edit onto the kept copy that lacks them, in the write that hides it, and undo takes them back. Each folder set that holds exact copies — two folders that mirror each other, or one folder with files saved twice — is one header whose rule decides every exact-copy group under it (keep the copies in one folder, or the original name, the oldest, the largest) and no file-of-one-photo group; a click on a photo or a name keeps that copy and outranks the rule; a group whose work cannot be combined is decided only by a click. Keep All Copies is remembered per pair of paths (`duplicate_kept_pair`, schema v11), carried by Otos moves, purged with either copy, and undoable; a later copy of kept copies is the only one offered. The groups have their own owner (`DuplicatesProjection`), asked for a build only after a catalog change: the window publishes without waiting for a build, and a change of scope, filter or sort never starts one. The list always shows every active group: one found while the user looks joins the list and the count at once. A file of zero bytes is never a copy. What each group keeps is one computation (`DuplicateResolution`): the click, else the folder rule for exact copies, else the default. The bar's `Move N Copies to Trash` moves exactly the copies tagged Trash through the ordinary Move to Trash path, asking nothing; a group always keeps one copy. An Immich account's copy is never tagged Trash (ADR 0046 §8): whatever a click, rule or default chooses, it stays and only library copies move, the header ends `Otos never removes copies in “Family”` (alone when nothing moves), its Move to Trash Now is disabled, an account counts as one folder named by the account, and a folder header never offers to keep a library's side over an account's, its line saying why. Return and double-click open a group in Compare or Loupe (`.duplicateGroup`), Back to Duplicates returns; Photo ▸ Keep This Copy / Keep All Copies / Review Again / Move Duplicates to Trash act on the selected photos, and a photo's context menu carries the ordinary Move to Trash for that copy. The list is three sections by the attention a group needs (`DuplicatesLayout`, published with the groups): groups whose work cannot be combined, then files of one photo, each newest first by capture date, then exact copies under their folder set's header — the set with the most groups first, three or more folders last, largest first inside each, a header closed until clicked unless it is the only one and opened by a selection made elsewhere; a group's section follows its copies, so a click, a rule or Keep All Copies never moves it. A folder's header counts its groups, the bytes they would move and their reasons. Each group's default records why it was chosen — the keeper criterion that separated the kept copy from the copy it beat last (birth times at whole seconds), or that each library keeps its own — or which work conflicts and in which library; a waiting group's header names the conflict in words (DES-107), and the kind noun (`videos`, `photos`, `items`) follows what the groups hold. A kept group stays in place as one quiet header whose `Review Again` forgets its pairs, undoably by its own name. In a pair, unkeeping the kept copy keeps the other; ⌘⌫ on the list moves only the selected groups' extra copies, never a kept one; after the act the bar shows its receipt with Undo until a click or rule changes or the user leaves the list. The bar's L1 is what will happen (`221 copies go to the Trash · 53.9 GB`) over L3 the count of items with duplicates, and is that count only while nothing would move (DES-107); its button carries the count (`Move 221 Copies to Trash`); files of one photo rank by pixels and bytes whether or not they name a capture, each group laid out as Photos lays it out, its date and why it keeps what it keeps | APP-061 | Duplicates shall also list versions of the same photo — different files of one capture, keeping the camera original with the most pixels by default — when their stored-thumbnail render signatures nearly match (difference hash within 10 bits, 32 × 32 grayscale mean difference below 4) and no capture fact sets them apart: two files that both name a camera and an instant group only when the model and the whole-second instant agree, the body serial agrees where both carry one, and both records carry the same sub-seconds — a record without sub-seconds proves nothing, so the pair does not group; a file without capture metadata joins only the one original clearly closer than any other; a RAW never groups with a processed file; a thumbnail an Immich account drew never pairs with one Otos rendered; copies in an identical group are left to it; an Immich external library's asset is never compared, since it names a file a library holds; versions in two libraries or accounts pair only while the user compares across them (APP-083) (ADR 0042). The compared stills and their record's pairing columns are read from two indexes that hold only those (v67), never from the wide rows; a build pairs again only when the stills, the identical copies or the reach moved, reads a still's evidence (render and sub-seconds) only when its stamp moved, and reads the copies a group lists for its members every build, so what it publishes is what a build from nothing publishes. A build is asked for by rows shown, Live Photo grouping, copies, choices, and a write of the date, kind, record, image or edit of a row Duplicates compares or lists (`DuplicatesHoldSQL`); the same write to a row it cannot see asks for none. Each file shows as its own photo, tagged Keep or Trash, and Return opens two side by side in Compare with the group as the filmstrip (DES-107). | Given two burst frames with identical pixels but different sub-seconds, no group; given a RAW and its JPEG, none; given an account's Immich-drawn thumbnail and a library's Otos rendering of one capture, none, while two Immich-drawn ones pair; given an original and an upscaled stripped copy, the original is kept; given a build with nothing changed, nothing is paired and no evidence read; given a choice on a member, its copy changes and the pairing is held; given a member's thumbnail redrawn, then signed again, or its camera changed, each build equals one from nothing; | APP-062 | An Immich account shall be a library root of kind `immichAccount` (ADR 0046 §2, §3): named by `immich:<normalized server>#<user id>`, its path an Otos staging folder under Caches (`RemoteStaging` in the build's own container, `StagingContainers`: the one value the Store derives from its catalog location and the camera monitor, sweeps, quit check and import release all read) derived from its identity and re-derived at every load, so a root stored at another build's or the old shared container moves into this build's own with every overlay its assets hold (a camera's root likewise), its server address kept in `library_root.remote_base_url` (migration `v15_library_root_remote_base_url`) and shown as its Settings location. It is listed in the sidebar's Libraries section and spanned by All Media, Places and Duplicates; it is never removable, an import destination or source, reorganized, scanned, watched, Erased or moved to the Trash, and enrichment never claims its rows to download an original (it reads their metadata in ranges, APP-066). Its rows stay browsable with no server: an account is available at launch from its stored listing. The same account added twice is one root; removing it deletes its rows and its staging folder and nothing on the server. | APP-063 | An Immich account's rows shall follow its server's sync stream (ADR 0046 §5), read by `ImmichCatalogSync` over `ImmichClient` with a login session whose token lives in the Keychain (`ImmichKeychainSessionStore`), never SQLite. An image or video in the timeline or archive is a row at `<asset id>/<original file name>` with its EXIF size; audio and other types are never rows. A Live Photo's motion part (`hidden`) is a row in its still's folder, `<still id>/<motion original file name>`, so the pairing rule pairs them (ADR 0041): listed whenever its still is, from the motion's own line in that pass (with its size when that is there too; a later size reaches it by its ID) or else from the server's `GET /api/assets/{id}`, hidden with its still, refreshed from its own line where it already lives in its still's folder when that line comes without its still's, purged when its still no longer links it or is deleted, and on its own permanent deletion; a motion part the server refuses to describe (400 or 404) leaves its still listed alone, the rows already in the still's folder kept, and the batch acknowledged; a video once listed in a folder of its own that becomes a motion part leaves that folder, whether its `hidden` line comes in a later batch or after its timeline line in the same one, so it is one row, in its still's folder. An asset line whose type or visibility this version does not know is ignored, logged and acknowledged, never a pass that cannot be read. Every row stores the source's own ID for it (`source_item_id`, migration v20), which names every request for its bytes and images and every size and deletion the stream reports apart from its line; a still's folder is never parsed for it. An asset in Immich's trash stays as a row hidden by its source (`hidden_by_source`, migration v16) with its overlay kept, and shows again when restored; a line without a size keeps the stored size; a permanent deletion, or a move into the locked folder, purges the asset's rows and overlay. Lines are applied in batches of 500 under a listing lease and acknowledged only after the Store applied them, so a dropped connection replays what it did not apply. A full resync is recorded as owed (`library_root.resync_owed`, migration v41, the revision of the newest request) when the server resets the cursor, before the reset is acknowledged, and when a new session is signed in (APP-064); while one is owed every pass asks the server to discard the cursor (`reset: true`) and reads the stream from zero, so a resync that dropped after acknowledging batches is never resumed from them, and the pass that reaches the end purges every row it did not list and settles the revision it started from in one Store transaction, leaving a request made meanwhile owed; a reset in a stream read from zero, or a second one in a pass, fails it (`docs/research/2026-11-13-immich-sync-reset.md`). A root that cannot get or keep its listing lease ends the pass as such, and the pass is retried; only a cancelled loop is a stopped pass. A pass is a sync only when its stream ends with `SyncCompleteV1`; a stream cut short keeps what it applied and is not reported as a sync. Server failures are classified by URL error code and HTTP status (`unreachable`, `unauthorized`, `refused`), never by text. A folder a batch lists in full purges only the rows inside it that the batch does not list, never a sibling folder or file whose name begins the same, and each such lookup reads only that folder's rows, so a batch's cost does not grow with the account (a 500-folder batch on a 69,000-row account went from about 7 s to milliseconds). Each pass receives the stream whole before applying it, so applying it never slows its transfer until the request times out. | Given a pass with images, a video, audio, an unlinked motion part and a locked asset, only the image and video are rows, with their sizes, and the newest ack of each type is sent once after the apply; given a trashed then restored asset, it is hidden then shown with its curation choice; given a deletion line, its row is gone; given a size in a later pass, the row gets it; given a still and the motion part it links, both are rows in the still's folder, each with its own size and ID; given a still whose motion part's lines came in an earlier pass, the motion is listed from the server's facts, takes a later size by its own ID, and is purged when the still stops linking it or is deleted; given a trashed still, its motion part is hidden with it; given a motion part's own line alone with a later modification time and size, its row takes them and stays hidden with its trashed still; given the motion part deleted alone, the still stays; given the v20 migration, existing account rows take their folder as their ID and no other kind gets one; given a reset, the listing read from zero purges what it no longer names; given a resync that drops after its first batch was acknowledged by a server that resumes from acknowledgements, the next pass reads from zero again and purges only the row the server no longer lists; given a resync requested while one runs, completing the first leaves the second owed; given a root with no lease, the pass says so rather than stopped; given a video listed alone that becomes a motion part, in a later batch or the same one, it is one row in its still's folder; given a motion part's line without its size, its still lists it with no request and the size arrives later; given a motion part the server refuses, its still is listed, the batch acknowledged, and the motion row already in its folder keeps its overlay; given an asset line of an unknown type or visibility, it is ignored; given a connection dropped after one batch, only that batch is acknowledged and the next pass applies the rest; given the client, login, refusals, transport failures, the line stream and acks behave as Immich 3.3.6 does; given the Keychain, a token round-trips and is removed.; given rows in `a/`, `a/sub/`, `a-b/`, `a2/` and `a.jpg`, a batch listing `a/` in full with one of its rows purges only the other rows of `a/` and `a/sub/`; given a stream received and its session then cancelled, every line of it is still read, and a refused stream is an error | Otos/Services/ImmichClient.swift, Otos/Services/ImmichCatalogSync.swift, Otos/Services/ImmichSessionStore.swift, Otos/Models/CatalogModels.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift, | APP-064 | Settings ▸ Libraries shall add an Immich account through one sheet (Add Immich Account…: server address, email, password; DES-041, DES-052, DES-053): Add checks the address, that the server speaks Immich 3, and the login, and says which failed (not an address, nothing answered, not Immich, another major version, wrong email or password) in the sheet's one footnote line, which otherwise says Otos never changes anything on the server, so the sheet keeps one height. An added account's session is kept in the Keychain and its sync runs at once, at each launch, after each pass on a poll while its server answers, and on a growing backoff while it does not (`ImmichAccounts`). Each account's status — connecting, online, offline, signed out — is what its server last answered: only a request that reached no server makes it offline, while a server that answered with a failure, or a failure of Otos's own, leaves it standing and retries on the backoff, and a report from a pass whose loop was replaced (signing in again) or stopped is dropped; a pass is online as soon as its first batch is applied and acknowledged, not only when it ends, so a first sync of a large library is online while it runs; Settings' Status column says when the account last synced rather than whether it is connected — `Syncing…` while a pass whose server answered applies the stream and then fetches the server's images, `Synced 3m ago` (`just now`, `yesterday`, then the date) since the last pass applied its listing to the stream's end (recorded then, not when its images are all fetched, which on a first sync takes hours), the same with a `wifi.slash` glyph and the tooltip `Can’t reach <host>. Last synced … Otos shows the photos it has stored.` while the server does not answer, `Signed Out`, and before any finished pass `Connecting…` or `Can’t reach server` — redrawn each minute; the last finished pass is kept in the catalog (`library_root.last_synced_at`, migration v34), so a launch whose server does not answer still says it; and the sidebar marks an offline or signed-out account with a glyph and its reason (DES-029). A signed-out account signs in again through the same sheet with its server filled in (Sign In…). Every new session, the first or one signed in again, owes a full resync (APP-063), because its empty cursor gets no reset from the server and proves nothing about rows it does not list: its first pass reads the stream from zero and purges a row the server no longer lists, such as one deleted while signed out. A pass whose root could not get or keep its listing lease is retried on the backoff; only a loop torn down rings no further pass. While Otos holds an account, signed in or not, it claims the account's staging tree (`StagingLocation.claimOwnership(of:)`), so another instance's sweep never deletes its staged originals; forgetting the account releases it. Staging containers are named by the running app's bundle identifier, so a dev build and the installed app never share one (ADR 0030). Removing an account stops its sync, logs its session out when the server answers, and deletes its Keychain item before its root is removed. | Given a server that answers, Add saves the session, adds the account and syncs its asset; given a wrong password, an old major version, an address nothing answers, or a non-http address, no account and no session remain; given the server answering, then not reachable, then answering, then refusing the session, the status is online, offline, online, signed out; given any status, a 5xx, an unreadable line, a reset loop, a catalog failure, a pass with no lease or a stopped pass leaves it as it was; given an account signed in again, its pass asks the server to read from zero, purges a row the server no longer lists, and settles the resync; given an account held signed out, a sweep leaves its staged original, and after forgetting it the sweep collects it; given a stream that fails after its second batch, the pass reports its server answered once, after its first batch's acknowledgement, and a stream that fails before any batch reports nothing; given a launch, an account with a saved session syncs and one without is signed out; given forgetting an account, its session is logged out and deleted.; given a pass, its listing is reported applied before any of its images is fetched and a failed stream reports none; given a pass that applied its listing, its time is kept, and a later launch whose server fails is offline with that same last sync and not syncing; given each status, Settings reads `Connecting…`, `Can’t reach server`, `Syncing…`, `Signed Out`, `Synced just now`, `3m ago`, `yesterday` | Otos/Services/ImmichAccounts.swift, Otos/Services/ImmichCatalogSync.swift, Otos/Services/StagingLocation.swift, Otos/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelImmich.swift, Otos/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelMutation.swift, Otos/Views/ImmichAccountSheetView.swift, Otos/Views/SettingsView.swift, Otos/Views/SidebarView.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogSchema.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift | `OtosTests/ImmichAccountsTests.swift#addingAnAccountSavesItsSessionAndSyncsIt`, `OtosTests/ImmichAccountsTests.swift#aRefusedAddLeavesNoAccount`, `OtosTests/ImmichAccountsTests.swift#statusFollowsWhatTheServerLastAnswered`, `OtosTests/ImmichAccountsTests.swift#signingInAgainResyncsFromZero`, `OtosTests/DeviceStagingRegistryTests.swift#anImmichAccountHoldsItsTreeUntilItIsForgotten`, `OtosTests/ImmichAccountsTests.swift#onlyAServerThatDoesNotAnswerIsOffline`, `OtosTests/ImmichCatalogSyncTests.swift#aPassReportsItsServerAnsweredBeforeItEnds`, `OtosTests/ImmichAccountsTests.swift#startResumesSavedSessionsAndSignsOutTheRest`, `OtosTests/ImmichAccountsTests.swift#forgettingAnAccountEndsItsSession`, `OtosTests/ImmichAccountsTests.swift#theLastSyncIsKeptAcrossLaunches`, `OtosTests/ImmichCatalogSyncTests.swift#aPassReportsItsListingBeforeItFetchesImages`, `OtosTests/ImmichAccountsTests.swift#anAccountsStatusSaysWhenItLastSynced` |
| APP-065 | After each sync pass, an Immich account's rows that have never been edited, have no Otos rendering and have no source images for their current file shall get them from the server (ADR 0046 §7, ADR 0035): Immich's own preview is downloaded, once per asset, and Otos's preview (stills only, at the server's size, never upscaled) and 256 px grid thumbnail are drawn from it and written through the rendering's staging and promotion, stamped as source products (`source_products_size`/`_mtime`, migration v17) and never as the rendering. The rows owed them are found from an index of exactly those rows (v65), so a pass over an account whose images are current reads none. A file that changes is owed new images, which replace the shown ones when they arrive; a preview that is no image is skipped and asked for again next pass while the rest are still fetched; an asset the server lists without a thumbhash (one whose previews it has not made, and every Live Photo motion part, which Immich never makes previews of) is not asked for its images, and one whose preview it answers with a 404, its answer that it has none, is skipped and recorded (`source_products_absent`, migration v39), so neither is asked for them again until a later listing of it carries a thumbhash; any other status (a 5xx, a 429, a refused session) is the server failing now, not an answer about the asset: it ends the fetch after the page's images already fetched are written, and the rest stay owed; the server not answering, or the images' volume at its free-space floor, ends the fetch the same way. An edited still always shows Otos's rendering, so it is never owed the server's images and none is fetched for it, whatever its file does; the write refuses the server's images for any row not owed them. | Given an account with a still and a video, a pass fetches each preview once: the still gets a preview at the server's size and a 256 px thumbnail, the video only a thumbnail, and neither stamps a rendering; A launch with nothing changed reads no vector. A pass is asked for only by a change People reads: of a photo, its date, kind, grouping or images only when it holds a face (`CatalogChangedFacts.faceTiles`), so dates and renders of photos nobody is in wake nothing. In the gallery, Add Name…/Rename…, Set Birthday…, Add to Favorites/Remove from Favorites, Merge Into ▸ (asked first), dragging one person onto another (asked first), Hide and Unhide each act through one intent; a hidden person opens nothing and leaves the filter, and is merged neither away nor into by any path (drag, Merge Into, a taken name: `CatalogPeopleState.mergeSourceRefusal`, `Ian is a hidden person`), its cell not draggable. Opening a person shows their photos as a leaf under People (`SidebarSelection.person`): every fixed library's photos of them (whether a row holds the person is one seek by person and photo, schema v54, as for the People filter and the likeness sort, never a walk of every face of the person), the People row highlighted, titled with their name, keeping the filter, with Back to People (which keeps it, and exists only in that leaf) and a toolbar menu of their settings; hiding them returns to the gallery, and merging them shows the person they became. Saving a name another visible person has keeps the birthday typed and asks `Are these the same person?`, merging on Merge; the answer submits the merge without judging it again, so the catalog judges it after any act ahead of it in the People stream, the window's selection, shown person and filter follow the merged person only once the catalog confirms it — a People state that no longer lists the person merged away, arriving before that confirmation, leaves them shown and filtered, and the confirmation takes the window to the person merged into (`PendingPeopleActs`); a redo of the merge is that merge and lands the same way — and a refusal there shows `Couldn’t Merge These People` with the disabled act's reason; Someone Else… with such a name gives the faces to that person, and with a new name makes one person with the name and birthday typed, one act. The sheet's Save is disabled with its reason (DES-038) whenever the save could not happen: an empty name, an unreadable birthday, photos from before the birthday, a hidden person's name, a taken name whose merge or move the hard rules refuse (that act's reason); every count in a refusal says what it holds, photos, videos or photos and videos, each item once (`MediaCount`), or for Someone Else… a birthday other than the named person's. The gallery's context menu, the menu bar's Photo menu items for the selected person (no keys of their own; disabled while no person is selected), the toolbar menu of a person's photos (its Merge Into ▸ offering the named people only) and each cell's VoiceOver actions are one `PersonMenu` the view model builds, a cell's built only as its menu or VoiceOver opens it; no cell's rendering reads more than its own person, so a People update or a selection change re-renders only the cells whose person or selection changed, the published People state carrying the visible and the hidden people, the names the sheet suggests and each person's index, derived off the main actor, and the toolbar menu of a person's photos built once per People state or scope; the gallery draws its focus ring, and Up and Down keep the column within the visible and the hidden sections' own rows (`PeopleGalleryStep`). The Person sheet says who it names and how many photos Save changes over a row of up to eight of the faces, and suggests the visible named people as the name is typed. Every count says what it holds (`MediaCount`): `photos`, `videos` when all are videos, and `photos and videos` (`Photos & Videos` in a menu) once both are, so a library video with a face counts in its person's total; a video's face is never outlined, its box being one frame's, and offers no Make Key Photo (ADR 0047). The face acts say who and how many (DES-113): the photo menus group the targeted photos' faces under each visible person — a named person's `Sam ▸` (`Sam · 3 Photos ▸` with several photos) holding Change to with the five people the faces most likely are then Someone Else…, This Is Not Sam, This Is Not a Face and with one photo Make Key Photo for Sam; an unnamed person's `Unnamed Person · 14 Photos ▸`, counting every photo of them, holding Name This Person with the five likeliest named people (a merge, asked first) then New Name…, and This Is Not a Face, with Not the Same Person only in that person's own photos — and the unidentified faces under Assign to: one `Unidentified Face ▸` per face of one photo, and across several photos one `Unidentified Faces · 3 Photos ▸`, whose choices give each photo's face most like the person, only when no photo holds more than one; a lone entry's acts sit at the top level under a section heading with its title, the list a submenu, wherever the menu opens. Highlighting a face item in a photo menu, or pointing at an Info pane row, outlines the faces it acts on wherever their photos show (the loupe and both compare panes, through the edit their decode shows and whether or not Info is open, and grid and filmstrip tiles; DES-113). The Info pane's People section renders the same groups and items (APP-041), with a link-style Add Name… and every photo of them in place of a missing name; where only text fits a person without a name is `Unnamed Person`. Every act is undoable on the window's undo stack, named by its item and the photos it changed (`Undo Name 3 Photos`, `Undo This Is Not Sam`). | Given Sam with three photos and a favourite with one, | APP-081 | A scope that spans the libraries and accounts (`SidebarSelection.showsOneCopy`: All Media, Places and its map areas, People and a person, On This Day, All Imports, and search within them) shall show one copy of each set of identical copies — rows with the same current SHA-1, in two libraries, a library and an account, or one folder — and one library's scope, an import batch's, Duplicates and its groups, and a map stack's members shall show every file (ADR 0054, owner 2026-11-14). Two files with the same digest in one library are one photo too: All Media shows the first by path, the library's own view shows both, Duplicates lists them as one group within their library (ADR 0042), and a choice on either is written on both (APP-089). The shown copy is a library's before an account's, then the first by root and path, among present copies; `media_asset.shown_copy_root_id` names it and three SQLite triggers keep it in the transaction of every write that moves a digest, its stamps, a file's size or mtime, its presence or its path (v60). A row is left out when its shown copy's root is in the scope; a row whose shown copy's root is not (its library not available) is left out when a copy ranked before it is present in the scope, read through the digest index for those rows alone, so with three or more copies only the next best reachable copy shows, and the preferred copy shows again when its library returns (owner, 2026-11-15), with no write on mount or unmount. An uploaded Immich asset's `checksum` (Base64 SHA-1) is its row's digest, stamped with the facts its listing wrote, and a size reported later fills a stamp listed without one; an external library's asset (`libraryId` set), whose checksum hashes its path, states none of its bytes. An external asset is instead a library file's copy when the account mirrors that library (`immich_mirror`): after each completed pass, the server prefix is learned from one asset's `originalPath`, which its own path checksum must confirm. The asset's path checksum must equal the SHA-1 of `path:`, the prefix and the file's relative path, and the server must list it with the size and whole-second modification time the file's digest was read at. It then takes the file's digest, in the file's digest write or at the end of the pass that lists it (v61). A digest write names `.copies`, which the combined scopes, People and Duplicates are built from, only when a row it wrote joined or left a digest another copy holds, or a mirrored asset took the file's digest (`DigestStanding`); a digest no other copy holds names only the row's own detail, and one written again unchanged names nothing. | Given a digest no other file holds, the write names `.detail` alone; given the second file with a digest, `.detail` and `.copies`; given the same digest written again, no change; given a library file and an account asset listed with its SHA-1, or an external asset at the file's mirrored path with its size and mtime, All Media shows the library's file and not the asset, the account's own scope shows the asset, and Duplicates shows both for an uploaded asset; given the library unavailable, All Media shows the asset; given a library file and two account copies with the library unavailable, All Media shows and counts only the first account copy, and the library's once it is back; given an external-library asset, it is shown; given two copies in one account and the first hidden by Immich's trash, the second is shown; given a digest listed before its size, it stays current once the size arrives; | APP-089 | A Like, Dislike or named face shall belong to the photo, not the copy (ADR 0054, ADR 0047; owner, 2026-11-15), over the copies the shown copy is chosen among (the same current SHA-1 in a library or an account, a mirror pair included). A choice act on any copy is written on every copy, whatever its presence, in the same transaction (trigger `media_asset_choice_made`), so every read of a choice — the order under a choices filter, counts, On This Day, the stacks, the tile badge on whichever copy shows, Info — reads the photo's; undo writes the previous choice back as an act. Each act takes the next order of `choice_sequence` (`media_choice_sequence`, v64); when a copy joins a photo every copy takes the choice of the latest act among them, and choices made before v64 rank before every act, Liked before Disliked before Not Rated. Deleting a copy leaves the choice on the others. A person's photos, People and the People filter find a photo by a face on any present copy (`PhotoFaceSQL`, through `has_copies` and the digest index), whether the person's photos are sought or the order walked, and a person's photo count counts the photo once (`PeopleSnapshot.photos`). Each copy is read for its own faces from its own preview (they are not the same pixels: Otos's 2560 px rendering, Immich's 1440 px preview), and every surface resolves a photo's faces through the same copies (`PhotoFaceSQL.presentCopiesOfDigest`, asked for each digest of a file with a face and a present copy, read with its faces; and People's unchecked tally counts a photo once while any present copy of it is unread. | Given a library file and its account copy, liking the account's copy likes both, a Liked filter finds the library's in All Media and in its library, and undo un-likes both; given a v62 catalog whose copies disagree, v64 gives Liked and Disliked Liked, Disliked and Not Rated Disliked; given a face only on a copy no combined scope shows, the person (sought and walked), People and the People filter find the photo and count it once; | APP-090 | The window shall say what its catalog's open is doing (DES-121). Store applies the pending migrations one at a time and reports, as the newest value of one stream (`CatalogStore.openings`), the step running and how many — known before the first runs, the registrations less the identifiers the catalog records — and the libraries and accounts the catalog holds, counted before any migration with SQL every schema version answers; it finishes the stream when the open ends. A click on one while the catalog opens selects it and keeps the rows. Every migration and every other step of the open is logged with its cost (`op=catalog.migration`, `op=catalog.database.open`, `op=catalog.start`'s `migrations` and `migrationsMs`), and migrations run with foreign keys enforced as they run, with no whole-catalog check after each (ADR 0002). | Given a catalog two migrations behind, the open runs them as steps 1 of 2 and 2 of 2 and then none; before the load each scope's title is its loaded title, or `Otos` for a library or person; in a hosted window, one spinning indeterminate indicator, drawn light and dark; the status is one height in every state. | Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift, Otos/Models/CatalogModels.swift, Otos/Services/SidebarSelectionPolicy.swift, Otos/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift, Otos/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelMap.swift, Otos/Views/ScopeWaitingView.swift, Otos/ContentView.swift | `OtosTests/CatalogStoreSchemaMigrationTests.swift#pendingMigrationsRunOneAtATimeAndSayWhichOfHowMany`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreSchemaMigrationTests.swift#theOpenReportsTheLibrariesItHoldsBeforeUpdating`, `OtosTests/BrowserPlaceTests.swift#whileTheCatalogOpensTheWindowSaysWhatItIsDoing`, `OtosTests/BrowserPlaceTests.swift#theOpeningRowsAreTheLibrariesTheOpenRead`, `OtosTests/ScopeWaitingWindowTests.swift#theWindowWhileTheCatalogOpens`, `OtosTests/ScopeWaitingWindowTests.swift#theOpeningStatusHoldsOneHeight`, `OtosTests/WindowStateApplyTests.swift#everyScopeHasItsTitle`; ## Media Enrichment

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance example | Implemented in | Tested by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ENR-001 | Every cataloged image and video shall be enriched in the background with a thumbnail and parsed media metadata, prioritizing what is in the viewport, then connected/removable source work in fallback claiming, and otherwise following the default newest-first browser usefulness order. | Given a library of materialized and dataless media, the background coordinator brings every asset to enriched, materializing dataless files first when none are local; given the user scrolls, visible assets are claimed ahead of background work (ENR-018) and priority claiming skips rows already claimed; given no visible priority candidate is available and removable sources are connected, fallback claims offer removable-source assets before fixed-library assets; given fallback claims within the same source class, they prefer newest best-capture-date assets before stable path tie-breakers rather than path order. | Otos/Services/EnrichmentCoordinator.swift, Otos/Stores/CatalogStore.swift | `OtosTests/EnrichmentCoordinatorTests.swift#visibleThumbnailLifecycleEnrichesViewportPriorityAssets`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreEnrichmentClaimTests.swift#fallbackEnrichmentClaimsRemovableSourcesBeforeFixedLibraries`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreEnrichmentClaimTests.swift#fixedSourcesShareTheClaimWindow`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreEnrichmentClaimTests.swift#fallbackEnrichmentClaimsUseNewestBestDateOrder`, `OtosTests/CatalogStoreEnrichmentClaimTests.swift#priorityClaimingSkipsRowsAlreadyClaimed` |

| ENR-008 | Otos shall eagerly request every dataless media file for background enrichment through `MaterializationCoordinator`, accepting the disk growth macOS has not yet evicted. A background- or device-priority download that would leave its volume below `FreeSpaceFloor` (5 GB, checked against its volume's `FreeSpaceLedger`, which counts the downloads already committed there without counting twice the ones the figure shows) is not started and finishes `insufficientLocalSpace`; a cloud row then keeps its claim, held by the enrichment scheduler (not outstanding, so it holds back no other row), and is offered again after the 60 s space recheck, and interactive and command downloads never wait for the floor. Interactive work outranks command work (user-commanded, off-screen: Move to Trash downloads), which outranks device work, which outranks background work; command runs under the bounded background persistence (120 s, fail-fast on provider refusal), is exempt from the background per-root cap, and like interactive stops its transfer when its last waiter leaves; background uses at most 15 global slots and one active transfer per root, while all priorities share the 2 GiB byte budget and two-large-video cap. A dataless file whose provider definitively refuses with `ETIMEDOUT` (domain-qualified `ETIMEDOUT`) twice consecutively on one transfer short-circuits that transfer in ~2s (`k 2` probes) with outcome `providerUnavailable` distinct from `timedOut`; two consecutive `providerUnavailable` on one root at device/background priority opens a 60s per-root `providerBreakerOpen` suppression so the next background candidates on that root are not dispatched (preventing a 32×2s storm behind `backgroundPerRootCap=1`). The breaker never suppresses interactive demand; an interactive success closes an open breaker for that root, an interactive failure neither opens nor extends one. | Given eager work from one root, only one physical background transfer is active; given interactive demand, no new lower-priority transfer starts until interactive demand drains; given a background transfer holding a root's cap and queued background work, a command request on that root is admitted next; given a stalled provider request that never returns `ETIMEDOUT`, the one-URL mechanism re-requests once per second within 120 seconds; given a provider that returns `ETIMEDOUT` twice on one file, that transfer finishes in ~2s with `providerUnavailable` and the second `providerUnavailable` on that root suppresses further background dispatch on that root for 60s with `reason=providerBreakerOpen`; given a volume 1,000 bytes above the floor, a 2,000-byte background or device download is not started while a 500-byte background, an interactive and a command download are, and a volume that reports no capacity admits; given a download that found its file already local while another is in flight, its bytes are given back to the ledger and the next download is admitted on them; given a figure that rises while downloads are in flight, the freed space is admitted; given a command whose provider refuses twice, it ends `providerUnavailable` (bounded persistence); given the last waiter of an interactive or a command transfer leaves, the transfer ends and its accounting returns to zero; given a held cloud download, its claim is released and the ledger offers it again after the recheck. | Otos/Services/MaterializationCoordinator.swift, Otos/Services/MaterializationAdmissionPolicy.swift, Otos/Services/EnrichmentCoordinator.swift, Otos/Services/EnrichmentSchedule.swift, Otos/Services/FileMaterialization.swift, Otos/Models/MediaOperationModels.swift | `OtosTests/FileMaterializationTests.swift#providerUnavailableShortCircuitsAfterTwoConsecutiveProbes`, `OtosTests/MaterializationCoordinatorProviderBreakerTests.swift#twoConsecutiveProviderUnavailableOpensBreakerAndSuppressesNextFile`, `OtosTests/MaterializationCoordinatorTests.swift#backgroundIsLimitedToTheRootCap`, `OtosTests/EnrichmentCoordinatorTests.swift#ejectDrainIgnoresPureInteractivePreviewTraffic`, `OtosTests/MaterializationCoordinatorTests.swift#commandIsAdmittedPastTheBackgroundRootCap`, `OtosTests/MaterializationCoordinatorTests.swift#backgroundAndDeviceDownloadsKeepTheFreeSpaceFloor`, `OtosTests/MaterializationCoordinatorTests.swift#aFileAlreadyLocalGivesItsRoomBackWhileOthersAreInFlight`, `OtosTests/FreeSpaceLedgerTests.swift#freedSpaceIsAdmittedBeforeTheStretchEnds`, `OtosTests/FreeSpaceLedgerTests.swift#aFigureThatHoldsStillNeverRebases`, `OtosTests/MaterializationCoordinatorTests.swift#aCommandRunsUnderTheBoundedBackgroundPersistence`, `OtosTests/MaterializationCoordinatorTests.swift#theLastWaiterLeavingEndsTheTransferAndReleasesAccounting`, `OtosTests/EnrichmentScheduleTests.swift#cloudDownloadHeldByTheFloorKeepsItsClaimAndRechecks`, `OtosTests/EnrichmentScheduleTests.swift#aRowWithNoSlotIsHeldAndHandedOffWhenOneFrees`, `OtosTests/EnrichmentCoordinatorTests.swift#anEmptyClaimIsNotAskedAgainUntilItsInputsMove`, `OtosTests/BackgroundOwnersIdleTests.swift#everyBackgroundOwnerIsQuietOnceItsWorkIsDone` |
| ENR-009 | `EnrichmentCoordinator` shall own only cursorless catalog claiming, a bounded 32-item working window, eight-worker local enrichment admission, one claim-lifecycle state per identity, and batched result application; `MaterializationCoordinator` shall own the separate bounded 16-transfer physical scheduler. Enrichment may hand off at most 16 materialization requests globally and two per root (one admitted background transfer plus one look-ahead request); a claim holds while handed-off work waits, with no expiry, until released or until the process ends, and loading the catalog clears every claim the last process left. Background materialization failure shall retry in-window through a capped exponential schedule and, once that schedule is exhausted, release its claim-window slot and be held, with its claim, for the pacing (900 s) before a fresh set of retries; a claimed row the hand-off has no slot for — whether routed from a claim or found dataless by its read — is held the same way and handed off when a slot frees; every held demand also ends when its row is deleted, moved to the Trash or left awaiting presence, when its root is removed or stops being enrichable (its claim given back), or, for a pacing hold, when its source size changes, and a transfer running when its row or root goes ends withdrawn, neither retried nor held, so no row a full hand-off, the free-space floor or a failing download holds is claimed and given back to rediscover it, and a background claim that found nothing is not asked again until a Store change or a claim given back moves its inputs (an empty claim is a write transaction); a row whose source sends the original only for the user's work is paced through a bounded process-local ledger (identity, earliest reclaim time, attempt count, source-size fragment of the `(size, mtime)` signature) that holds no work payload and resets on success, source-signature change, or process restart; cancellation shall release a claim without consuming a retry attempt. Admission diagnostics aggregate rejected candidates by reason and emit only on full state change plus one unblocked transition, truthfully reporting a blocked state — never a spurious unblocked — while effective interactive demand defers device/background dispatch. Root drain shall drop work claimed but not started and wait, without a time limit, only for work this coordinator started — enrichment, and every download it handed to the shared coordinator, which counts as running until its result returns — never for unrelated interactive preview traffic under the same root. | Given 25,000 dataless assets whose provider transfers are held, the enrichment working window stays at or below 32, its shared-materializer handoff stays at or below 16 globally and two per root, and the physical scheduler keeps one same-root background transfer active; given blocked video enrichment, later admissible still work dispatches and unchanged wakes do not repeat the blocked diagnostic; given success, the old claim releases, the identity re-enters through `freshly`, and any ledger entry clears; given a permanently-failing background population, each asset releases its claim-window slot after the in-window retry schedule exhausts and is held, with its claim, for the pacing, so a fresh candidate is still claimable and none is claimed again meanwhile; given a row deleted, or a library removed, while its demand is held for the pacing, no transfer is spent on it once the pacing passes; given a row found dataless at the outstanding limit, it is held, never one more outstanding; given a cancelled (non-shutdown) materialization, the claim releases without scheduling a retry or consuming ledger pacing; given a pending or effectively in-flight interactive waiter defers lower-priority dispatch, the blocked diagnostic is recorded truthfully and does not clear to unblocked while that demand holds; given an eject drain with only unrelated interactive preview traffic active under the root, the drain returns without waiting for it; given running enrichment and queued claims under the root, the drain drops the queued claims and returns when the running enrichment finishes; | ENR-010 | Enrichment shall do no media I/O or thumbnail decoding on the MainActor: the coordinator and pools run off-main, the controller and loupe/compare pane thumbnail fallback load and decode store misses off-main, preheating warms decoded thumbnails off-main into tile store, and store hits paint synchronously from immutable decoded images while keeping video-badge formatting small and synchronous in tile configuration from a store-cached duration string. | Given a cold enrichment sweep, no media bytes or store-miss thumbnail decode run on the MainActor; given a tile configures, duration/fallback badge text can update synchronously without restarting image work and filename/date formatting does not run in the tile path; given tile store already holds the incoming image for that asset key, the tile assigns that immutable image synchronously; given the store misses, thumbnail loading/decoding runs in a detached task that applies the decoded image into tile store on Main under a stale-key guard; given preheat warms a thumbnail, decode runs off-main and partially cancelled AppKit IDs are filtered before merge/warm; given tile loads and store hits run, no media I/O or store-miss decode hops onto the MainActor. | Otos/Services/EnrichmentCoordinator.swift, Otos/Services/MediaEnricher.swift, Otos/Views/MediaAssetTileView.swift, Otos/Views/TileContentStore.swift, Otos/Views/MediaGridBridge.swift, Otos/Views/PanePresentation.swift, Otos/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift | `OtosTests/TileContentStoreTests.swift#mergeDetailInsertsAndRequestsDecodeWhenThumbnailExists`, `OtosTests/TileContentStoreTests.swift#applyDecodedRejectsStaleKey`, `OtosTests/MediaGridPaintInvariantTests.swift#detailQueryMergeDoesNotBlankPaintedVisibleTile` |
| ENR-011 | Connected-source enrichment priority shall be implemented at claim/staging selection time without a durable second queue: available removable roots are claimed before fixed roots, and the fixed roots share what is left of each claim window, a share each and then the room a root did not fill to the others in turn, so a root whose rows wait on downloads (a cloud library's videos) never holds back one whose rows are readable now (an Immich account's, read in ranges); and MTP eager staging submits still/image items before videos, then capture date, then path, at shared materializer device priority, and **at most one device transfer runs at a time**. ImageCaptureCore funnels every request through one serial `ICDeviceCommandQueue`, so concurrent device requests are not parallelism — they queue inside a framework Otos cannot observe, time, or cancel, and they cost throughput. The cap is per-transport, never global, so cloud and local materialization stay concurrent; and it is enforced at the device session as well as in admission, because import copies call the session directly and never pass through the materializer. Readiness deferrals that have elapsed are dropped when the pass ends, and every wait the scheduler holds — download retries, ledger pacing, readiness rechecks, the catalog-write retry — wakes it through one deadline, the earliest still ahead of the instant the pass started from, so a wait no pass reached can never make the scheduler wake itself at zero delay. A device that is proven to answer ranged reads is **not staged for enrichment at all**: its rows are enriched from the container's own index, a few kilobytes per item, so transferring them first would move the whole card to read a date that sits a few kilobytes into each file. Capability is measured once per attach — one 12-byte read whose magic must identify a family consistent with the item's extension — because `GetPartialObject` is optional in MTP and devices that advertise it are documented failing it. A device that fails that probe stages exactly as before: every offered seed is staged, seeds beyond the outstanding-materialization limit are held in memory and admitted as each download completes, never discarded. Staging also remains the fallback for a single item a ranged read cannot serve — an unindexable container, a repeatedly failing read, or a loupe open — through the same per-item materialization path. A ranged read that fails is transient, never a definitive absence of a capture date, so a detached camera is retried rather than named from its filesystem mtime, and a read that never answers times out rather than parking its thread. | ENR-027 | Every asset with a stored thumbnail, in any available root, shall get a search embedding read from that thumbnail by the bundled SigLIP 2 model (ADR 0048), never from its original, newest capture first: a pass finds what is owed in one walk of the window's newest-first date order (`CandidateWalk`, ADR 0009), and a change that names its rows is reconciled by key and its rows (with their Live Photo partners) are embedded and read first, and the backlog walk keeps its place across passes, stopping at its next page for a change, so a photo made owed now is embedded before an older backlog without the library being read again; only the first pass and a change that names no rows reconcile the whole library and start the walk from the newest. A pass that leaves an embedding or a text reading owed that it could not make (a model that would not load, a prediction or a write that failed) tries again after a backoff, 30 s doubling to 15 minutes, with no catalog change. The tokenizer reproduces the trained one's ids, and the int8 text path reproduces the full-precision embeddings. The index keeps its vectors in one file pair in Application Support, mapped read-only (ADR 0048), as Float16 slots with each slot's loose labels kept as numbered names and their stored confidences, deciding the label gate exactly as the parsed labels would. The catalog names the file and how many slots its embeddings commit (v70), in the same commit as the rows; a launch with a file that is intact, of the model's revision and holds the committed slots maps it and reads no stored vector, cutting off any slot past the count, and any other file (missing, torn, short of the count) is rebuilt from the catalog's current embeddings in pages of 2,000, each its own read, while a query sees the empty index. A later slot for an asset supersedes its earlier one, and once dead slots reach a quarter of the live (and 1,024) the live ones are compacted into a new pair the catalog then names; memory takes every file, append and dead slot in one step with the revision an answer carries (`SearchIndexMemory`), so a query after a compaction is answered from the new file's slots. The free-space floor is asked before the catalog commits slots and never by the write after it; a write that fails after its commit keeps memory answering from its slots until a rebuild completes, and a rebuild removes the other files only once the catalog names its own, removing just its own when it fails. The process claims the folder (`flock`) before it opens or builds a file, so one writer holds it, and a launch that opens the current file removes any other pair a crash left. | ENR-029 | A still the renderer cannot decode shall record the decoder that could not read it (`undecodable_by`: Otos's decode revision and the macOS build, `ImageRenderer.decoderIdentity`), alongside the rendering that fell back to QuickLook's thumbnail (ENR-016). At launch, every still another still decoder could not read loses its rendering stamp and its verdict, so enrichment renders it again, and the count released is logged (`op=catalog.start phase=undecodableReleased stills=`); a video's verdict is its frame reader's (ENR-031); the hand-back seeks those stills through `idx_media_asset_undecodable` rather than scanning every row (`OtosTests/CatalogStoreQueryPlanTests.swift#placeCellAndUndecodableReadsAreIndexDriven`). | ENR-030 | Every located item shall be named for the Places filter (ADR 0051): the map is cut into cells 0.02° on a side, generated by SQLite from the stored primary location into indexed columns (`asset_metadata.place_cell_latitude` / `place_cell_longitude`) exactly as Swift computes them, the north pole and the 180th meridian in the cell below them so every cell's center is a valid coordinate, and `PlaceNameReader` asks Apple Maps once per cell, from the cell's center, never an item's own coordinate, for its country and its town with context, stored with the Mac's language and script (`PlaceNameLanguage`, never its region); the catalog keeps one language's names, so a drain in a new language forgets the others' and names every cell again, and a change of region alone asks nothing; names stored under a full locale before v43 are re-keyed to its language by v45, so an upgrade asks nothing already named in the Mac's language. A cell is stored with no names only for an answer that there are none (`placemarkNotFound`, no map item, a refused coordinate); any other outcome stores nothing, is logged, and the reader waits (2 s doubling to 5 min), goes on to the next cell and asks the failed cell again in another pass. The unnamed cells are read through the place-cell index; the filter's places walk the scope's rows (a wide scope's date order index), each located row's cell found by its coordinate in `idx_asset_metadata_location`. | ENR-032 | Every still of a library or an Immich account shall be described by Apple's on-device language model (ADR 0053), never through Private Cloud Compute: once its faces are read from its stored preview and its text and scene labels are current, `PhotoDescriptionWriter` hands the model that preview at 1,024 px (never the original, so nothing is downloaded) and `PhotoDescriptionPrompt`'s facts — the capture day and part of day, the map cell's town and country, each named face's position left to right with its person's name and age on that day (whole years, months under two, about from a birth year alone) and whether anyone else in it is unnamed (a face given to no one, an unnamed or a hidden person; faces carried onto the preview through its edit, one the crop leaves out not told) — with instructions made for the photo: a photo naming no one is described with instructions that never mention names or ages, one whose faces are all named asks for each by name and no other, and only one with unnamed faces too says everyone else stays unnamed; a named photo with no age given is told to state no age, one with ages to give no other, the certain text and the confident labels, each only from a reading current for the still's preview and thumbnail as they are now (`PhotoDescriptionStorage.readingsSQL`, ADR 0048) — and stores its answer in `photo_description` (v58) with a source naming the preview, the faces' stamp, each face's person, name, birthday and hidden flag, the place names, the capture time and the prompt revision, so any change to them owes a new description, and a write lands only while the source still matches. A declined answer is stored as declined; a failed preview or request writes nothing and is passed over for 15 minutes; an unavailable model stops the drain for 15 minutes and a busy one for a minute (`time: backoff`). One request at a time, newest capture first: the owed stills are found in one newest-first walk (`CandidateWalk`, ADR 0009), and a fact change during it restarts the walk from the newest still at its next page, so an import is described before an older backlog. | Given a still whose faces, text and labels are read, it is owed a description; given a declined answer, the Info pane reads declined; given an unavailable model, nothing is written and the still stays owed; given a crop, only the faces inside it are told, placed on the cropped picture;
